(Z_{1}^{(u_{1})},Z_{2}^{(u_{2})},\ldots,Z_{k-1}^{(u_{k-1})},Z_{k}^{(\overline{u_{k}})},Z_{k+1}^{(u_{k+1})},\ldots,Z_{2N}^{(u_{2N})})
. More precisely

\mathcal{Z}_{k,i}(u)\coloneqq\begin{cases}Z_{2N}^{(u_{2N})}Z_{2N-1}^{(u_{2N-1})}\cdots Z_{i}^{(u_{i})}&\text{if $k<i$,}\\[10.0pt]
Z_{2N}^{(u_{2N})}Z_{2N-1}^{(u_{2N-1})}\cdots Z_{k}^{(\overline{u_{k}})}\cdots Z_{i}^{(u_{i})}&\text{if $k\geq i$.}\end{cases}

(4.142)

3.

\hat{X}_{k,i}=\sum_{u=(u_{i},\ldots,u_{2N})\in\{0,1\}^{2N-i+1}}\mathcal{Z}_{k,i}(u)X_{k}\mathcal{Z}_{i}(u).

We also write
\mathcal{Z}_{2N+1}(u)\coloneqq\mathbbm{1}
,
\mathcal{Z}_{k,2N+1}(u)=\mathbbm{1}
, and
\hat{X}_{k,2N+1}=X_{k}
for all
k\in[2N]
.

By direct calculation, we find that for
i\in[2N+1]
and
k\in[2N]
,

\hat{X}_{k,i}=\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}_{k,i}(\sigma^{X}_{k}\otimes\mathbbm{1})\mathcal{V}_{k,i},

(4.143)

where
\mathcal{V}_{k,i}
is an isometry defined in the next paragraph.
Eq.

4.143
allows us to deduce that

\|\hat{X}_{k,i}\|_{\infty}\leq 1,

(4.144)

which will be crucial in the following main argument when we use the replacement lemma (
Lemma

2.6
).

For
i\in[2N]
and
k\in[2N]
, we define the isometry
\mathcal{V}_{k,i}
according to whether
k<i
or
k\geq i
. If
k\geq i
, we define
\mathcal{V}_{k,i}
by the following circuit:

1.

Initialize
2N
ancilla qubits to
\ket{0}
.

2.

Apply
H^{\otimes(2N-i+1)}
on the
i
-to-
2N
-th ancilla qubits.

3.

Apply
(2N-i+1)
c-
Z_{j}
gates where the control is by the
j
-th ancilla qubit and
j=i,\ldots,2N
.

4.

Apply
H^{\otimes(2N-i+1)}
on the
i
-to-
2N
-th ancilla qubits.

5.

Apply a single c-
X_{k}
gate where the control is by the
k
-th ancilla qubit.

If
k<i
, we define
\mathcal{V}_{k,i}
by the same circuit except, in the second step, we additionally apply an
H
gate on the
k
-th ancilla qubit. We also define
\mathcal{V}_{k,2N+1}\coloneqq\mathbbm{1}
for all
k\in[2N]
.

We illustrate
\mathcal{V}_{k,i}
when
2N=4
,
k=1
, and
i=3
below.

\ket{0}
\ket{0}
\ket{0}
\ket{0}
\ket{\psi}
H
H
H
Z_{3}
Z_{4}
H
H
X_{1}

Figure 4:
Illustration of
\mathcal{V}_{k,i}
when
2N=4
,
k=1
, and
i=3
.

We proceed with our main argument. By direct calculation, we have

\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}(\sigma^{X}_{k}\otimes\mathbbm{1})\mathcal{V}=\sum_{u\in\{0,1\}^{2N}}\mathcal{Z}_{k,1}(u)X_{k}\mathcal{Z}_{1}(u).

(4.145)

Note that
\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}(\sigma^{X}_{k}\otimes\mathbbm{1})\mathcal{V}=\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}_{k,1}(\sigma^{X}_{k}\otimes\mathbbm{1})\mathcal{V}_{k,1}=\hat{X}_{k,1}
.

By direct expansion, we have

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}(\sigma^{X}_{k}\otimes\mathbbm{1})\mathcal{V}-X_{k}\|_{\sigma^{\theta}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Tr}[(\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}(\sigma^{X}_{k}\otimes\mathbbm{1})\mathcal{V}-X_{k})^{2}\sigma^{\theta}]

(4.146)

\displaystyle=1+\mathrm{Tr}[\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}(\sigma^{X}_{k}\otimes\mathbbm{1})\mathcal{V}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}(\sigma^{X}_{k}\otimes\mathbbm{1})\mathcal{V}\sigma^{\theta}]-2\real\mathrm{Tr}[\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}(\sigma^{X}_{k}\otimes\mathbbm{1})\mathcal{V}\,X_{k}\,\sigma^{\theta}]

\displaystyle=1+\mathrm{Tr}[(\mathcal{V}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger})(\sigma^{X}_{k}\otimes\mathbbm{1})\mathcal{V}\sigma^{\theta}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}(\sigma^{X}_{k}\otimes\mathbbm{1})]-2\real\mathrm{Tr}[\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}(\sigma^{X}_{k}\otimes\mathbbm{1})\mathcal{V}\,X_{k}\,\sigma^{\theta}]

\displaystyle\leq 2-2\real\mathrm{Tr}[\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}(\sigma^{X}_{k}\otimes\mathbbm{1})\mathcal{V}\,X_{k}\,\sigma^{\theta}],

where, in the last inequality, we used the fact that
\mathcal{V}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}
is a projector (Hermitian and idempotent) and
(\sigma^{Z}_{k}\otimes\mathbbm{1})\mathcal{V}\sigma^{\theta}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}(\sigma^{Z}_{k}\otimes\mathbbm{1})
is a positive semi-definite operator.

We now show that the last term,
\real\mathrm{Tr}[\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}(\sigma^{X}_{k}\otimes\mathbbm{1})\mathcal{V}\,X_{k}\,\sigma^{\theta}]
, is close to
1
. By the lifting lemma (
Lemma

2.16
, part
5
) and the efficiency of
\mathcal{V}
(
Lemma

4.31
), it suffices to consider the case when
\theta=k
. In the following, the terms {commute, replace, o.s.-commute} are shorthand for {
Proposition

4.21
,
Lemma

2.6
,
Proposition

4.29
} respectively (“o.s.” stands for “operator-state”). When using the replacement lemma, we crucially use
\|\hat{X}_{k,i}\|_{\infty}\leq 1
(
Eq.

4.144
).

\displaystyle\quad\mathrm{Tr}[\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}(\sigma^{X}_{k}\otimes\mathbbm{1})\mathcal{V}\,X_{k}\,\sigma^{k}]

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Tr}[\hat{X}_{k,1}\,X_{k}\,\sigma^{k}]

(
\hat{X}_{k,1}=\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}(\sigma^{X}_{k}\otimes\mathbbm{1})\mathcal{V}
)

\displaystyle=\sum_{u}\mathrm{Tr}[\mathcal{Z}_{k,1}(u)X_{k}\mathcal{Z}_{1}(u)\,X_{k}\sigma^{k}]

(
Eq.

4.145
)

\displaystyle=\sum_{a}\mathrm{Tr}\Bigl[Z_{1}^{(a)}\Bigl(\sum_{u=(u_{2},\ldots,u_{N})}\mathcal{Z}_{k,2}(u)X_{k}\mathcal{Z}_{2}(u)\Bigr)Z_{1}^{(a)}\,X_{k}\sigma^{k}\Bigr]

(definition of
\mathcal{Z}_{k,2}(u)
,
\mathcal{Z}_{2}(u)
)

\displaystyle=\sum_{a}\mathrm{Tr}[Z_{1}^{(a)}\hat{X}_{k,2}Z_{1}^{(a)}\,X_{k}\sigma^{k}]

(definition of
\hat{X}_{k,2}
)

\displaystyle\approx_{\sqrt{\gamma_{H}+{\mathrm{negl}(\lambda)}}}\sum_{a}\mathrm{Tr}[Z_{1}^{(a)}\hat{X}_{k,2}\,X_{k}Z_{1}^{(a)}\sigma^{k}]

(commute and replace)

\displaystyle\approx_{\sqrt{\gamma_{H}+{\mathrm{negl}(\lambda)}}}\sum_{a}\mathrm{Tr}[Z_{1}^{(a)}\hat{X}_{k,2}\,X_{k}\sigma^{k}Z_{1}^{(a)}]

(o.s.-commute and replace)

\displaystyle=\sum_{a}\mathrm{Tr}[Z_{1}^{(a)}\hat{X}_{k,2}\,X_{k}\sigma^{k}]

\displaystyle\hskip-3.0pt\begin{pmatrix}\text{trace is cyclic and}\\
\text{$(Z_{1}^{(a)})^{2}=Z_{1}^{(a)}$}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Tr}[\hat{X}_{k,2}\,X_{k}\sigma^{k}]

\displaystyle\text{$\Bigl(\sum_{a}Z_{1}^{(a)}=\mathbbm{1}\Bigr)$}.

Therefore, we have
\mathrm{Tr}[\hat{X}_{k,1}\,X_{k}\,\sigma^{k}]\approx_{\sqrt{\gamma_{H}}+{\mathrm{negl}(\lambda)}}\mathrm{Tr}[\hat{X}_{k,2}\,X_{k}\sigma^{k}]
. Using the above reasoning another
k-2
times and the triangle inequality gives

\mathrm{Tr}[\hat{X}_{k,1}\,X_{k}\,\sigma^{k}]\approx_{k\sqrt{\gamma_{H}}+{\mathrm{negl}(\lambda)}}\mathrm{Tr}[\hat{X}_{k,k}\,X_{k}\sigma^{k}]
,
where we use
k\cdot{\mathrm{negl}(\lambda)}\leq 2N\cdot{\mathrm{negl}(\lambda)}={\mathrm{negl}(\lambda)}
. Then by the definitions of
\hat{X}_{k,k}
and
\hat{X}_{k,k+1}
, and direct calculation,

\mathrm{Tr}[\hat{X}_{k,k}\,X_{k}\sigma^{k}]=\sum_{a}\mathrm{Tr}[Z_{k}^{(\overline{a})}\hat{X}_{k,k+1}Z_{k}^{(a)}\,X_{k}\sigma^{k}]
. Using
Z_{k}^{(a)}=(1+(-1)^{a}Z_{k})/2
to perform the sum over
a
gives

\mathrm{Tr}[\hat{X}_{k,k}\,X_{k}\sigma^{k}]=\frac{1}{2}(\mathrm{Tr}[\hat{X}_{k,k+1}X_{k}\sigma^{k}]-\mathrm{Tr}[Z_{k}\hat{X}_{k,k+1}Z_{k}X_{k}\sigma^{k}]).

(4.147)

We approximate both terms using the reasoning above another
2N-k
times and the triangle inequality (when handling the second term, we additionally use the commutativity of the
Z_{i}
s):

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{Tr}[\hat{X}_{k,k+1}X_{k}\sigma^{k}]-\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{Tr}[Z_{k}\hat{X}_{k,k+1}Z_{k}X_{k}\sigma^{k}]

(4.148)

\displaystyle\approx_{(2N-k)\sqrt{\gamma_{H}}+{\mathrm{negl}(\lambda)}}\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{Tr}[\hat{X}_{k,2N+1}X_{k}\sigma^{k}]-\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{Tr}[Z_{k}\hat{X}_{k,2N+1}Z_{k}X_{k}\sigma^{k}]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{Tr}[X_{k}X_{k}\sigma^{k}]-\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{Tr}[Z_{k}X_{k}Z_{k}X_{k}\sigma^{k}]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{Tr}[Z_{k}X_{k}Z_{k}X_{k}\sigma^{k}],

where the second equation is by
\hat{X}_{k,2N+1}=X_{k}
and the last equation is by
X_{k}^{2}=\mathbbm{1}
.

Applying the triangle inequality to all of the above approximations and then taking the real part gives

\real\mathrm{Tr}[\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}(\sigma^{X}_{k}\otimes\mathbbm{1})\mathcal{V}\,X_{k}\,\sigma^{k}]\approx_{N\sqrt{\gamma_{H}}+{\mathrm{negl}(\lambda)}}\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{4}(\mathrm{Tr}[Z_{k}X_{k}Z_{k}X_{k}\sigma^{k}]+\mathrm{Tr}[X_{k}Z_{k}X_{k}Z_{k}\sigma^{k}]).

(4.149)

By directly unpacking the definition of the anti-commutation relation
\{X_{k},Z_{k}\}\approx_{\sqrt{\gamma_{H}}+{\mathrm{negl}(\lambda)},\sigma^{k}}0
(
Proposition

4.22
), we see that

\mathrm{Tr}[Z_{k}X_{k}Z_{k}X_{k}\sigma^{k}]+\mathrm{Tr}[X_{k}Z_{k}X_{k}Z_{k}\sigma^{k}]\approx_{\sqrt{\gamma_{H}}+{\mathrm{negl}(\lambda)}}-2
(no replacement lemma used). Hence

\real\mathrm{Tr}[\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}(\sigma^{X}_{k}\otimes\mathbbm{1})\mathcal{V}\,X_{k}\,\sigma^{k}]\approx_{N\sqrt{\gamma_{H}}+{\mathrm{negl}(\lambda)}}1.

(4.150)

Lastly, recall from the comment below
4.5
that we assume
N\gamma_{H}
is non-negligible in
\lambda
,
so
O(N\sqrt{\gamma_{H}}+{\mathrm{negl}(\lambda)})=O(N\sqrt{\gamma_{H}})
. Hence the lemma.
∎

Lemma 4.33
.

Let
D
be an efficient perfect device. For
k\in[N]
and
\theta\in[2N]\cup\{0,\diamond\}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}(\sigma^{X}_{k}\otimes\sigma^{Z}_{N+k}\otimes\mathbbm{1})\mathcal{V}\approx_{N^{1/4}\gamma_{H}^{1/8},\sigma^{\theta}}\widetilde{X}_{k}\widetilde{Z}_{N+k},

(4.151)

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}(\sigma^{Z}_{k}\otimes\sigma^{X}_{N+k}\otimes\mathbbm{1})\mathcal{V}\approx_{N^{1/4}\gamma_{H}^{1/8},\sigma^{\theta}}\widetilde{Z}_{k}\widetilde{X}_{N+k}.

Proof.

By
Lemma

2.10
and
Lemma

4.32
, for all
k\in[2N]
and
\theta\in[2N]\cup\{0,\diamond\}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}\widetilde{Z}_{k}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}

\displaystyle\approx_{\sqrt{\gamma_{H}}+{\mathrm{negl}(\lambda)},\mathcal{V}\sigma^{\theta}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}}\sigma^{Z}_{k}\otimes\mathbbm{1},

(4.152)

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}\widetilde{X}_{k}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}

\displaystyle\approx_{\sqrt{N}\gamma_{H}^{1/4},\mathcal{V}\sigma^{\theta}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}}\sigma^{Z}_{k}\otimes\mathbbm{1}.

It suffices to prove the first equation for
k=1
as the proof for other
k
is analogous. In the following, the terms {indist., lift, o.s.-commute, replace} are shorthand for {
Lemma

4.11
,
Lemma

2.16
(part
5
),
Proposition

4.29
,
Lemma

2.6
} respectively.

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}[(\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}(\sigma^{X}_{1}\otimes\sigma^{Z}_{N+1}\otimes\mathbbm{1})\mathcal{V}-\widetilde{X}_{1}\widetilde{Z}_{N+1})^{\dagger}(\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}(\sigma^{X}_{1}\otimes\sigma^{Z}_{N+1}\otimes\mathbbm{1})\mathcal{V}-\widetilde{X}_{1}\widetilde{Z}_{N+1})\sigma^{\theta}]

\displaystyle=2-2\real\mathrm{Tr}[\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}(\sigma^{X}_{1}\otimes\sigma^{Z}_{N+1}\otimes\mathbbm{1})\mathcal{V}\widetilde{X}_{1}\widetilde{Z}_{N+1}\sigma^{\theta}]

(rearrange)

\displaystyle\simeq_{{\mathrm{negl}(\lambda)}}2-2\real\mathrm{Tr}[\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}(\sigma^{X}_{1}\otimes\sigma^{Z}_{N+1}\otimes\mathbbm{1})\mathcal{V}\widetilde{X}_{1}\widetilde{Z}_{N+1}\sigma^{1}]

(indist. and lift)

\displaystyle\approx_{\sqrt{\gamma_{H}}+{\mathrm{negl}(\lambda)}}2-2\real\mathrm{Tr}[\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}(\sigma^{X}_{1}\otimes\sigma^{Z}_{N+1}\otimes\mathbbm{1})\mathcal{V}\widetilde{X}_{1}\sigma^{1}\widetilde{Z}_{N+1}]

(o.s.-commute and replace)

\displaystyle=2-2\real\mathrm{Tr}[(\mathcal{V}\widetilde{Z}_{N+1}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger})(\sigma^{X}_{1}\otimes\sigma^{Z}_{N+1}\otimes\mathbbm{1})(\mathcal{V}\widetilde{X}_{1}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger})(\mathcal{V}\sigma^{1}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger})]

(trace is cyclic and
\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}\mathcal{V}=\mathbbm{1}
)

\displaystyle\approx_{\gamma_{H}^{1/4}+{\mathrm{negl}(\lambda)}}2-2\real\mathrm{Tr}[(\sigma^{X}_{1}\otimes\mathbbm{1}_{2}\otimes\mathbbm{1})(\mathcal{V}\widetilde{X}_{1}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger})(\mathcal{V}\sigma^{1}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger})]

(
Eq.

4.152
and replace)

\displaystyle=2-2\real\mathrm{Tr}[\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}(\sigma^{X}_{1}\otimes\mathbbm{1}_{2}\otimes\mathbbm{1})\mathcal{V}\widetilde{X}_{1}\sigma^{1}]

(trace is cyclic and
\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}\mathcal{V}=\mathbbm{1}
)

\displaystyle\approx_{\sqrt{\gamma_{H}}+{\mathrm{negl}(\lambda)}}2-2\,\real\mathrm{Tr}[\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}(\sigma^{X}_{1}\otimes\mathbbm{1}_{2}\otimes\mathbbm{1})\mathcal{V}\sigma^{1}\widetilde{X}_{1}]

(o.s.-commute and replace)

\displaystyle=2-2\real\mathrm{Tr}[(\mathcal{V}\widetilde{X}_{1}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger})(\sigma^{X}_{1}\otimes\mathbbm{1}_{2}\otimes\mathbbm{1})(\mathcal{V}\sigma^{1}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger})]

(trace is cyclic and
\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}\mathcal{V}=\mathbbm{1}
)

\displaystyle\approx_{N^{1/4}\gamma_{H}^{1/8}}2-2\real\mathrm{Tr}[(\mathbbm{1}_{2}\otimes\mathbbm{1}_{2}\otimes\mathbbm{1})(\mathcal{V}\sigma^{1}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger})]

(
Eq.

4.152
and replace)

\displaystyle=0.

Therefore, by the triangle inequality, we have

\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}(\sigma^{X}_{k}\otimes\sigma^{Z}_{N+k}\otimes\mathbbm{1})\mathcal{V}\approx_{N^{1/4}\gamma_{H}^{1/8},\sigma^{\theta}}\widetilde{X}_{k}\widetilde{Z}_{N+k},

(4.153)

and hence the first equation of the lemma.

The proof for the second equation is similar. We may again only consider the case
k=1
. Then, the second equation follows from the same steps as above except we lift to
\theta=N+1
at the lifting step.
∎

4.8
States under the swap isometry

In this subsection, we show that the states
\sigma^{\theta,v}
of a device that passes our protocol with high probability must be close to states that we call
\tau^{\theta,v}
under the swap isometry. The states
\tau^{\theta,v}
are defined as follows.

Definition 4.34

(density operators
\tau^{\theta,v}
)
.

Let
v\in\{0,1\}^{2N}
. For
\theta\in[2N]\cup\{0,\diamond\}
, we define the
2N
-qubit density operator
\tau^{\theta,v}\coloneqq|\tau^{\theta,v}\rangle\langle\tau^{\theta,v}|
, according to the following three cases.

\ket{\tau^{\theta,v}}\coloneqq\begin{cases}\ket{v_{1}}\otimes\cdots\otimes\ket{v_{\theta-1}}\otimes\ket{(-)^{v_{\theta}}}\otimes\ket{v_{\theta+1}}\otimes\cdots\otimes\ket{v_{2N}}&\text{if $\theta\in[2N]$,}\\
\ket{v}\coloneqq\ket{v_{1}}\otimes\cdots\otimes\ket{v_{2N}}&\text{if $\theta=0$,}\\
\ket{\psi^{v}}&\text{if $\theta=\diamond$,}\end{cases}

(4.154)

where
\ket{\psi^{v}}
is as defined in
Eq.

3.5
.

For convenience, we also define the following notation. For
a\in\{0,1\}
and
k\in[2N]
, we define

\displaystyle|a\rangle\langle a|_{k}\coloneqq\mathbbm{1}_{2}^{\otimes(k-1)}\otimes|a\rangle\langle a|\otimes\mathbbm{1}_{2}^{\otimes(2N-k)},

(4.155)

\displaystyle|(-)^{a}\rangle\langle(-)^{a}|_{k}\coloneqq\mathbbm{1}_{2}^{\otimes(k-1)}\otimes|(-)^{a}\rangle\langle(-)^{a}|\otimes\mathbbm{1}_{2}^{\otimes(2N-k)}.

For
v\in\{0,1\}^{2N}
and
j\in[2N]
, we define

\displaystyle|v_{j:2N}\rangle\langle v_{j:2N}|\coloneqq\mathbbm{1}_{2}^{\otimes(j-1)}\otimes|v_{j}\rangle\langle v_{j}|\otimes\ldots\otimes|v_{2N}\rangle\langle v_{2N}|,

(4.156)

\displaystyle|(-)^{v_{j:2N}}\rangle\langle(-)^{v_{j:2N}}|\coloneqq\mathbbm{1}_{2}^{\otimes(j-1)}\otimes|(-)^{v_{j}}\rangle\langle(-)^{v_{j}}|\otimes\ldots\otimes|(-)^{v_{2N}}\rangle\langle(-)^{v_{2N}}|.

For
i,\theta\in[2N]
and
v\in\{0,1\}^{2N}
, we define

\displaystyle\chi^{\prime}(\theta,i,v)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\lVert X_{i}-\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}(\sigma^{X}_{i}\otimes\mathbbm{1})\mathcal{V}\rVert^{2}_{\sigma^{\theta,v}},

(4.157)

\displaystyle\chi_{\diamond}^{\prime}(i,v)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\lVert\widetilde{X}_{i}\widetilde{Z}_{N+i}-\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}(\sigma^{X}_{i}\otimes\sigma^{Z}_{N+i}\otimes\mathbbm{1})\mathcal{V}\rVert^{2}_{\sigma^{\diamond,v}},

\displaystyle\zeta_{\diamond}^{\prime}(i,v)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\lVert\widetilde{Z}_{i}\widetilde{X}_{N+i}-\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}(\sigma^{Z}_{i}\otimes\sigma^{X}_{N+i}\otimes\mathbbm{1})\mathcal{V}\rVert^{2}_{\sigma^{\diamond,v}},

so that, by definition,

\displaystyle X_{i}

\displaystyle\approx_{\chi^{\prime}(\theta,i,v),\sigma^{\theta,v}}

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}(\sigma^{X}_{i}\otimes\mathbbm{1})\mathcal{V},

(4.158)

\displaystyle\widetilde{X}_{i}\widetilde{Z}_{N+i}

\displaystyle\approx_{\chi_{\diamond}^{\prime}(i,v),\sigma^{\diamond,v}}

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}(\sigma^{X}_{i}\otimes\sigma^{Z}_{N+i}\otimes\mathbbm{1})\mathcal{V},

\displaystyle\widetilde{Z}_{i}\widetilde{X}_{N+i}

\displaystyle\approx_{\zeta_{\diamond}^{\prime}(i,v),\sigma^{\diamond,v}}

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}(\sigma^{Z}_{i}\otimes\sigma^{X}_{N+i}\otimes\mathbbm{1})\mathcal{V}.

Similarly to our use of
\chi
and
\zeta
introduced in
4.8
,
we will use
\chi^{\prime}
,
\chi_{\diamond}^{\prime}
, and
\zeta_{\diamond}^{\prime}
to bound distances between the output states of our swap isometry and the ideal states in
Lemmas

4.35
and
4.37
.

By
Lemma

4.32
and
Lemma

4.33
, we see that, for all
i,\theta\in[2N]
,

\sum_{v}\chi^{\prime}(\theta,i,v)=O(N\sqrt{\gamma_{H}}),\quad\sum_{v}\chi_{\diamond}^{\prime}(i,v)=O(N^{1/4}\gamma_{H}^{1/8}),\quad\text{and}\quad\sum_{v}\zeta_{\diamond}^{\prime}(i,v)=O(N^{1/4}\gamma_{H}^{1/8}).

(4.159)

With the above definitions in place, we can prove the following lemma.

Lemma 4.35
.

Let
D
be an efficient perfect device. For all
\theta\in[2N]\cup\{0\}
and
v\in\{0,1\}^{2N}
, there exists a positive semi-definite operator
\alpha^{\theta,v}\in\mathrm{Pos}(\mathcal{H})
such that

\sum_{v}\|\mathcal{V}\sigma^{\theta,v}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}-\tau^{\theta,v}\otimes\alpha^{\theta,v}\|_{1}\leq O(N^{3/2}\gamma_{H}^{1/4}).

(4.160)

Proof.

It suffices to prove the lemma for
\theta=1
as the proof for other
\theta
is analogous (the proof for
\theta=0
is also simpler). First, we prove the following claim using
Lemma

4.32
.

Claim 4.36
.

1.

There exists a positive semi-definite operator
\beta^{v}\in\mathrm{Pos}(\mathcal{H})
, such that

\lVert\mathcal{V}\sigma^{1,v}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}-|(-)^{v_{1}}\rangle\langle(-)^{v_{1}}|\otimes\beta^{v}\rVert_{1}\leq\epsilon(v),

(4.161)

where
\epsilon(v)\coloneqq O\Bigl(\bigl(\chi(1,v)+\sqrt{\mathrm{Tr}[\sigma^{1,v}]\cdot\chi^{\prime}(1,1,v)}\bigr)^{1/2}\sqrt{\mathrm{Tr}[\sigma^{1,v}]}\Bigr)
.

2.

For all
k\in[2N]
with
k\geq 2
, we have
\lVert|v_{k}\rangle\langle v_{k}|_{k}\otimes\mathbbm{1}-\mathbbm{1}\rVert_{\mathcal{V}\sigma^{1,v}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}}^{2}=\zeta(1,k,v)
.

Proof of
4.36
.

To prove the first part, consider

\displaystyle\lVert|(-)^{v_{1}}\rangle\langle(-)^{v_{1}}|_{1}\otimes\mathbbm{1}-\mathbbm{1}\rVert_{\mathcal{V}\sigma^{1,v}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Tr}[\mathcal{V}\sigma^{1,v}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}]-\mathrm{Tr}[(|(-)^{v_{1}}\rangle\langle(-)^{v_{1}}|_{1}\otimes\mathbbm{1})\mathcal{V}\sigma^{1,v}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}]

(4.162)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Tr}[\sigma^{1,v}]-\mathrm{Tr}[\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}(|(-)^{v_{1}}\rangle\langle(-)^{v_{1}}|_{1}\otimes\mathbbm{1})\mathcal{V}\sigma^{1,v}]

\displaystyle\leq\mathrm{Tr}[\sigma^{1,v}]-\mathrm{Tr}[X_{1}^{(v_{1})}\sigma^{1,v}]+O(\sqrt{\mathrm{Tr}[\sigma^{1,v}]\cdot\chi^{\prime}(1,1,v)})

\displaystyle=\chi(1,v)/4+O(\sqrt{\mathrm{Tr}[\sigma^{1,v}]\cdot\chi^{\prime}(1,1,v)}),

where the inequality uses the replacement lemma (
Lemma

2.6
) with the definition of
\chi^{\prime}(1,1,v)
in
Eq.

4.157
and
Lemma

2.11
, and the last equality is by
Eq.

4.45
.

Using
Lemma

2.8
with the above equation, we obtain

\|\mathcal{V}\sigma^{1,v}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}-(|(-)^{v_{1}}\rangle\langle(-)^{v_{1}}|_{1}\otimes\mathbbm{1})\mathcal{V}\sigma^{1,v}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}(|(-)^{v_{1}}\rangle\langle(-)^{v_{1}}|_{1}\otimes\mathbbm{1})\|_{1}\leq\epsilon(v),

(4.163)

where

\epsilon(v)\coloneqq O\Bigl(\bigl(\chi(1,v)+\sqrt{\mathrm{Tr}[\sigma^{1,v}]\cdot\chi^{\prime}(1,1,v)}\bigr)^{1/2}\sqrt{\mathrm{Tr}[\sigma^{1,v}]}\Bigr).

(4.164)

But
(|(-)^{v_{1}}\rangle\langle(-)^{v_{1}}|_{1}\otimes\mathbbm{1})\mathcal{V}\sigma^{1,v}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}(|(-)^{v_{1}}\rangle\langle(-)^{v_{1}}|_{1}\otimes\mathbbm{1})
is of the form
|(-)^{v_{1}}\rangle\langle(-)^{v_{1}}|\otimes\beta^{v}
for

\displaystyle\beta^{v}\coloneqq(\bra{(-)^{v_{1}}}_{1}\otimes\mathbbm{1})\mathcal{V}\sigma^{1,v}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}(\ket{(-)^{v_{1}}}_{1}\otimes\mathbbm{1}).

(4.165)

Hence the first part of the claim.

The second part is simpler. Using the first equation of
Lemma

4.32
and
Eq.

4.45
(
k\geq 2
), we see

\lVert|v_{k}\rangle\langle v_{k}|_{k}\otimes\mathbbm{1}-\mathbbm{1}\rVert_{\mathcal{V}\sigma^{1,v}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}}^{2}\\
=\mathrm{Tr}[\sigma^{1,v}]-\mathrm{Tr}[\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}(|v_{k}\rangle\langle v_{k}|_{k}\otimes\mathbbm{1})\mathcal{V}\sigma^{1,v}]\\
=\mathrm{Tr}[\sigma^{1,v}]-\mathrm{Tr}[Z_{k}^{(v_{k})}\sigma^{1,v}]=\zeta(1,k,v),

(4.166)

which completes the proof.

∎

We proceed to use the claim to establish the lemma. For all
k\in[2N]
with
k\geq 2
, consider

\displaystyle\||(-)^{v_{1}}\rangle\langle(-)^{v_{1}}|_{1}\otimes\beta^{v}-(|v_{k}\rangle\langle v_{k}|_{k}\otimes\mathbbm{1})\left(|(-)^{v_{1}}\rangle\langle(-)^{v_{1}}|_{1}\otimes\beta^{v}\right)\,(|v_{k}\rangle\langle v_{k}|_{k}\otimes\mathbbm{1})\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\||(-)^{v_{1}}\rangle\langle(-)^{v_{1}}|_{1}\otimes\beta^{v}-\mathcal{V}\sigma^{1,v}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}\|_{1}+\|\mathcal{V}\sigma^{1,v}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}-(|v_{k}\rangle\langle v_{k}|_{k}\otimes\mathbbm{1})\,\mathcal{V}\sigma^{1,v}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}\,(|v_{k}\rangle\langle v_{k}|_{k}\otimes\mathbbm{1})\|_{1}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad+\|(|v_{k}\rangle\langle v_{k}|_{k}\otimes\mathbbm{1})\,\mathcal{V}\sigma^{1,v}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}\,(|v_{k}\rangle\langle v_{k}|_{k}\otimes\mathbbm{1})-(|v_{k}\rangle\langle v_{k}|_{k}\otimes\mathbbm{1})\,|(-)^{v_{1}}\rangle\langle(-)^{v_{1}}|_{1}\otimes\beta^{v}\,(|v_{k}\rangle\langle v_{k}|_{k}\otimes\mathbbm{1})\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq 2\||(-)^{v_{1}}\rangle\langle(-)^{v_{1}}|_{1}\otimes\beta^{v}-\mathcal{V}\sigma^{1,v}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}\|_{1}+\|\mathcal{V}\sigma^{1,v}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}-(|v_{k}\rangle\langle v_{k}|_{k}\otimes\mathbbm{1})\,\mathcal{V}\sigma^{1,v}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}\,(|v_{k}\rangle\langle v_{k}|_{k}\otimes\mathbbm{1})\|_{1}

using the first part of
4.36
to bound the first term and the second part of
4.36
with
Lemma

2.8
to bound the second term:

\displaystyle\leq 2\epsilon(v)+2\sqrt{\zeta(1,k,v)\cdot\mathrm{Tr}[\mathcal{V}\sigma^{1,v}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}]}=2\epsilon(v)+2\sqrt{\zeta(1,k,v)\cdot\mathrm{Tr}[\sigma^{1,v}]}.

That is, for all
k\in[2N]
with
k\geq 2
, we have

\||(-)^{v_{1}}\rangle\langle(-)^{v_{1}}|_{1}\otimes\beta^{v}-(|v_{k}\rangle\langle v_{k}|_{k}\otimes\mathbbm{1})\,|(-)^{v_{1}}\rangle\langle(-)^{v_{1}}|_{1}\otimes\beta^{v}\,(|v_{k}\rangle\langle v_{k}|_{k}\otimes\mathbbm{1})\|_{1}\leq 2\epsilon(v)+2\sqrt{\mathrm{Tr}[\sigma^{1,v}]\cdot\zeta(1,k,v)}.

(4.167)

We then have

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{V}\sigma^{1,v}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}-(|v_{2:2N}\rangle\langle v_{2:2N}|\otimes\mathbbm{1})\,(|(-)^{v_{1}}\rangle\langle(-)^{v_{1}}|_{1}\otimes\beta^{v})\,(|v_{2:2N}\rangle\langle v_{2:2N}|\otimes\mathbbm{1})\|_{1}

using the triangle inequality:

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathcal{V}\sigma^{1,v}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}-(|(-)^{v_{1}}\rangle\langle(-)^{v_{1}}|_{1}\otimes\beta^{v})\|_{1}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\|(|(-)^{v_{1}}\rangle\langle(-)^{v_{1}}|_{1}\otimes\beta^{v})-(|v_{2:2N}\rangle\langle v_{2:2N}|\otimes\mathbbm{1})\,(|(-)^{v_{1}}\rangle\langle(-)^{v_{1}}|_{1}\otimes\beta)\,(|v_{2:2N}\rangle\langle v_{2:2N}|\otimes\mathbbm{1})\|_{1}

using
4.36
:

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon(v)+\|(|(-)^{v_{1}}\rangle\langle(-)^{v_{1}}|_{1}\otimes\beta^{v})

\displaystyle\quad\quad-(|v_{2:2N}\rangle\langle v_{2:2N}|\otimes\mathbbm{1})\,(|(-)^{v_{1}}\rangle\langle(-)^{v_{1}}|_{1}\otimes\beta^{v})\,(|v_{2:2N}\rangle\langle v_{2:2N}|\otimes\mathbbm{1})\|_{1}

using
Lemma

2.12
with
Eq.

4.167
:

\displaystyle\leq(2N-1)\,\epsilon(v)+2\,\sum_{k=2}^{N}\sqrt{\mathrm{Tr}[\sigma^{1,v}]\cdot\zeta(1,k,v)}.

Finally, using the properties of
\{\chi^{\prime},\chi,\zeta\}
given in
Eqs.

4.159
and
4.10
, the definition of
\epsilon(v)
in
Eq.

4.164
, and the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{v\in\{0,1\}^{2N}}\|\mathcal{V}\sigma^{1,v}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}-(|v_{2:2N}\rangle\langle v_{2:2N}|\otimes\mathbbm{1})\,(|(-)^{v_{1}}\rangle\langle(-)^{v_{1}}|_{1}\otimes\beta^{v})\,(|v_{2:2N}\rangle\langle v_{2:2N}|\otimes\mathbbm{1})\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq O\Bigl(N\sum_{v}\epsilon(v)+\sum_{v}\sum_{k=2}^{2N}\sqrt{\mathrm{Tr}[\sigma^{1,v}]\cdot\zeta(1,k,v)}\Bigr)

\displaystyle\leq O\Bigl(N\sqrt{\gamma_{H}}+N\,\sum_{v}\bigl(\chi(1,v)+\sqrt{\mathrm{Tr}[\sigma^{1,v}]\cdot\chi^{\prime}(1,1,v)}\bigr)^{1/2}\sqrt{\mathrm{Tr}[\sigma^{1,v}]}\Bigr)

\displaystyle\leq O\Bigl(N\sqrt{\gamma_{H}}+N\Bigl(\sum_{v}\chi(1,v)+\sqrt{\mathrm{Tr}[\sigma^{1,v}]\cdot\chi^{\prime}(1,1,v)}\Bigr)^{1/2}\Bigr)

\displaystyle\leq O\Bigl(N(\gamma_{H}+N\sqrt{\gamma_{H}}+{\mathrm{negl}(\lambda)})^{1/2}+N\sqrt{\gamma_{H}}\Bigr)\leq O(N^{3/2}\gamma_{H}^{1/4}).

Noting that
(|v_{2:2N}\rangle\langle v_{2:2N}|\otimes\mathbbm{1})\,(|(-)^{v_{1}}\rangle\langle(-)^{v_{1}}|_{1}\otimes\beta^{v})\,(|v_{2:2N}\rangle\langle v_{2:2N}|\otimes\mathbbm{1})
is of the form

\tau^{1,v}\otimes\alpha^{1,v}=|(-)^{v_{1}}\rangle\langle(-)^{v_{1}}|\otimes|v_{2}\rangle\langle v_{2}|\otimes\cdots\otimes|v_{2N}\rangle\langle v_{2N}|\otimes\alpha^{1,v},

(4.168)

for some positive semi-definite operator
\alpha^{1,v}
, establishes the lemma.

∎

Lemma

4.35
characterizes the states
\sigma^{\theta,v}
for
\theta\in\{0\}\cup[2N]
. For
\theta=\diamond
, we have

Lemma 4.37
.

Let
D
be an efficient perfect device. For all
v\in\{0,1\}^{2N}
, there exists a positive semi-definite operator
\alpha^{\diamond,v}\in\mathrm{Pos}(\mathcal{H})
such that

\sum_{v}\|\mathcal{V}\sigma^{\diamond,v}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}-\tau^{\diamond,v}\otimes\alpha^{\diamond,v}\|_{1}\leq O(N^{17/16}\gamma_{H}^{1/32}).

(4.169)

Proof.

The proof is similar to the proof of
Lemma

4.35
. We provide the details for completeness.

We will use the fact that, for all
v\in\{0,1\}^{2N}
, we have

|\psi^{v}\rangle\langle\psi^{v}|\otimes\mathbbm{1}=\prod_{i=1}^{N}(\sigma^{Z}_{i}\otimes\sigma^{X}_{N+i}\otimes\mathbbm{1})^{(v_{i})}(\sigma^{X}_{i}\otimes\sigma^{Z}_{N+i}\otimes\mathbbm{1})^{(v_{N+i})}.

(4.170)

For all
i\in[N]
, we have

\displaystyle\lVert(\sigma^{X}_{i}\otimes\sigma^{Z}_{N+i}\otimes\mathbbm{1})^{(v_{N+i})}\otimes\mathbbm{1}-\mathbbm{1}\rVert^{2}_{\mathcal{V}\sigma^{\diamond,v}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}}

(4.171)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Tr}[\mathcal{V}\sigma^{\diamond,v}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}]-\mathrm{Tr}[(\sigma^{X}_{i}\otimes\sigma^{Z}_{N+i}\otimes\mathbbm{1})^{(v_{N+i})}\mathcal{V}\sigma^{\diamond,v}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}]

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Tr}[\sigma^{\diamond,v}]-\mathrm{Tr}[\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}(\sigma^{X}_{i}\otimes\sigma^{Z}_{N+i}\otimes\mathbbm{1})^{(v_{N+i})}\mathcal{V}\sigma^{\diamond,v}]

\displaystyle\leq\mathrm{Tr}[\sigma^{\diamond,v}]-\mathrm{Tr}[(\widetilde{X}_{i}\widetilde{Z}_{N+i})^{(v_{N+i})}\sigma^{\diamond,v}]+O(\sqrt{\mathrm{Tr}[\sigma^{\diamond,v}]\cdot\chi_{\diamond}^{\prime}(i,v)})

\displaystyle=\chi_{\diamond}(i,v)+O(\sqrt{\mathrm{Tr}[\sigma^{\diamond,v}]\cdot\chi_{\diamond}^{\prime}(i,v)}),

where the inequality uses the replacement lemma (
Lemma

2.6
) with the definition of
\chi_{\diamond}^{\prime}
in
Eq.

4.157
and
Lemma

2.11
, and the last equality is by
Eq.

4.45
.
Similarly, we have

\lVert(\sigma^{Z}_{i}\otimes\sigma^{X}_{N+i}\otimes\mathbbm{1})^{(v_{i})}\otimes\mathbbm{1}-\mathbbm{1}\rVert^{2}_{\mathcal{V}\sigma^{\diamond,v}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}}\leq\zeta_{\diamond}(i,v)+O\Bigl(\sqrt{\mathrm{Tr}[\sigma^{\diamond,v}]\cdot\zeta_{\diamond}^{\prime}(i,v)}\Bigr).

(4.172)

Using
Lemma

2.8
with
Eqs.

4.171
and
4.172
, we obtain

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{V}\sigma^{\diamond,v}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}-(\sigma^{X}_{i}\otimes\sigma^{Z}_{N+i}\otimes\mathbbm{1})^{(v_{N+i})}\mathcal{V}\sigma^{\diamond,v}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}(\sigma^{X}_{i}\otimes\sigma^{Z}_{N+i}\otimes\mathbbm{1})^{(v_{N+i})}\|_{1}\leq\epsilon_{1}(i,v),

(4.173)

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{V}\sigma^{\diamond,v}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}-(\sigma^{Z}_{i}\otimes\sigma^{X}_{N+i}\otimes\mathbbm{1})^{(v_{i})}\mathcal{V}\sigma^{\diamond,v}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}(\sigma^{Z}_{i}\otimes\sigma^{X}_{N+i}\otimes\mathbbm{1})^{(v_{i})}\|_{1}\leq\epsilon_{2}(i,v),

where

\displaystyle\epsilon_{1}(i,v)\coloneqq O\Bigl(\bigl(\chi_{\diamond}(i,v)+\sqrt{\mathrm{Tr}[\sigma^{\diamond,v}]\cdot\chi_{\diamond}^{\prime}(i,v)}\bigr)^{1/2}\sqrt{\mathrm{Tr}[\sigma^{\diamond,v}]}\Bigr),

(4.174)

\displaystyle\epsilon_{2}(i,v)\coloneqq O\Bigl(\bigl(\zeta_{\diamond}(i,v)+\sqrt{\mathrm{Tr}[\sigma^{\diamond,v}]\cdot\zeta_{\diamond}^{\prime}(i,v)}\bigr)^{1/2}\sqrt{\mathrm{Tr}[\sigma^{\diamond,v}]}\Bigr).

Using
Eq.

4.170
and
Lemma

2.12
with
Eq.

4.173
gives

\|\mathcal{V}\sigma^{\diamond,v}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}-|\psi^{v}\rangle\langle\psi^{v}|\mathcal{V}\sigma^{\diamond,v}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}|\psi^{v}\rangle\langle\psi^{v}|\|_{1}\leq\sum_{i=1}^{N}\epsilon_{1}(i,v)+\epsilon_{2}(i,v).

(4.175)

Therefore, summing over
v
gives

\sum_{v}\|\mathcal{V}\sigma^{\diamond,v}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}-|\psi^{v}\rangle\langle\psi^{v}|\mathcal{V}\sigma^{\diamond,v}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}|\psi^{v}\rangle\langle\psi^{v}|\|_{1}\leq\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{v}\epsilon_{1}(i,v)+\epsilon_{2}(i,v)\leq O(N^{17/16}\gamma_{H}^{1/32}).

(4.176)

But
|\psi^{v}\rangle\langle\psi^{v}|\mathcal{V}\sigma^{\diamond,v}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}|\psi^{v}\rangle\langle\psi^{v}|
is of the form
\tau^{\diamond,v}\otimes\alpha^{\diamond,v}
for some positive semi-definite operator
\alpha^{\diamond,v}
. Hence the first part of the lemma.

∎

Lastly, we show there is not much information about
\theta

and
v
in
\alpha^{\theta,v}
. The proof is similar to that of
[
GMP23
, Proposition 4.32 (arXiv version v2)]
but we include it for completeness
6
6

6

In v1 of this paper, we provided an independent proof of this lemma (see Lemmas 4.37 and 4.39 in v1) but it gave a worse bound. To be clear, we learned the main ideas of the current proof from
[
GMP23
, Proposition 4.32 (arXiv version v2)]
.
.

Lemma 4.38
.

Let
D
be an efficient perfect device. There exists a state
\alpha\in\mathrm{Pos}(\mathcal{H})
such that the following holds. For all
\theta\in[2N]\cup\{0,\diamond\}
and
v\in\{0,1\}^{2N}
, let
\alpha^{\theta,v}\in\mathrm{Pos}(\mathcal{H})
be as defined in
Lemmas

4.35
and
4.37
. Then, there exist numbers
\{\delta(v)\geq 0\mid v\in\{0,1\}^{2N}\}
such that

\alpha^{\theta,v}\overset{c}{\approx}_{\delta(v)}\alpha/2^{2N}
for all
v\in\{0,1\}^{2N}
and

\displaystyle\sum_{v\in\{0,1\}^{2N}}\delta(v)\leq O(N^{3/2}\gamma_{H}^{1/32}).

Proof.

In this proof, we write

\epsilon\coloneqq\max_{\theta\in[2N]\cup\{0,\diamond\}}\sum_{v}\|\mathcal{V}\sigma^{\theta,v}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}-\tau^{\theta,v}\otimes\alpha^{\theta,v}\|_{1}\leq O(N^{3/2}\gamma_{H}^{1/32}),

(4.177)

where the inequality uses
Lemmas

4.35
and
4.37
.

For all
\theta\in[2N]\cup\{0,\diamond\}
, we have

\lVert\mathcal{V}\sigma^{\theta}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}-\sum_{v}\tau^{\theta,v}\otimes\alpha^{\theta,v}\rVert\leq\sum_{v}\lVert\mathcal{V}\sigma^{\theta,v}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}-\tau^{\theta,v}\otimes\alpha^{\theta,v}\rVert+{\mathrm{negl}(\lambda)}\leq\epsilon+{\mathrm{negl}(\lambda)},

(4.178)

where the first inequality uses
Lemma

4.17
and the second inequality uses the definition of
\epsilon
.

Let
\alpha^{\diamond}\coloneqq\sum_{v}\alpha^{\diamond,v}
.
We first prove

\sum_{v}\tau^{0,v}\otimes\alpha^{0,v}\overset{c}{\simeq}_{2\epsilon+{\mathrm{negl}(\lambda)}}\sum_{v}\tau^{0,v}\otimes\alpha^{\diamond}/2^{2N}

(4.179)

by contradiction. Suppose
Eq.

4.179
does not hold. Then there exists an efficient POVM
\{\Lambda,\mathbbm{1}-\Lambda\}
such that

\mathrm{Tr}\Bigl[\Lambda\Bigl(\sum_{v}\tau^{0,v}\otimes\alpha^{0,v}-\sum_{v}\tau^{0,v}\otimes\alpha^{\diamond}/2^{2N}\Bigr)\Bigr]>2\epsilon+{\mu(\lambda)}

(4.180)

for some non-negligible function
{\mu(\lambda)}>0
.
Since the two states on either side of
Eq.

4.179
have a block-diagonal structure, we can without-loss-of-generality assume that
\Lambda
has the same structure. That is,

\Lambda=\sum_{v}\tau^{0,v}\otimes\Lambda_{v}\quad\text{for some $\Lambda_{v}$}.

(4.181)

Hence, our assumption in
Eq.

4.180
is equivalent to

\sum_{v}\mathrm{Tr}\Bigl[\Lambda_{v}(\alpha^{0,v}-\alpha^{\diamond}/2^{2N})\Bigr]\geq 2\epsilon+{\mu(\lambda)}.

(4.182)

Define a new POVM
\{\Gamma,\mathbbm{1}-\Gamma\}
by

\Gamma\coloneqq\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}\left(\sum_{v}\tau^{0,v}\otimes\Lambda_{v}\right)\mathcal{V},

(4.183)

which is efficient since
\mathcal{V}
is efficient.

Then, we obtain a contradiction as follows:

\displaystyle{\mathrm{negl}(\lambda)}

\displaystyle\geq\mathrm{Tr}[\Gamma(\sigma^{0}-\sigma^{\diamond})]

(
\sigma^{0}\overset{c}{\approx}\sigma^{\diamond}
)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Tr}\left[\left(\sum_{v}\tau^{0,v}\otimes\Lambda_{v}\right)\left(\mathcal{V}\sigma^{0}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}-\mathcal{V}\sigma^{\diamond}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}\right)\right]

(trace is cyclic)

\displaystyle\geq\mathrm{Tr}\left[\left(\sum_{v}\tau^{0,v}\otimes\Lambda_{v}\right)\left(\sum_{v}\tau^{0,v}\otimes\alpha^{0,v}-\sum_{u}\tau^{\diamond,u}\otimes\alpha^{\diamond,u}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\quad\quad-2\epsilon-2\,{\mathrm{negl}(\lambda)}

(
Eq.

4.178
)

\displaystyle=\sum_{v}\mathrm{Tr}[\Lambda_{v}\alpha^{0,v}]-\frac{1}{2^{2N}}\sum_{v}\mathrm{Tr}\Bigl[\Lambda_{v}\Bigl(\sum_{u}\alpha^{\diamond,u}\Bigr)\Bigr]-2\epsilon-2\,{\mathrm{negl}(\lambda)}

\displaystyle\hskip-3.0pt\begin{pmatrix}\text{$\mathrm{Tr}[\tau^{0,v}\tau^{\diamond,u}]=1/2^{2N}$}\\
\text{for all $u,v\in\{0,1\}^{2N}$}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=\sum_{v}\mathrm{Tr}[\Lambda_{v}(\alpha^{0,v}-\alpha^{\diamond}/2^{2N})]-2\epsilon-2\,{\mathrm{negl}(\lambda)}

(definition of
\alpha^{\diamond}
)

\displaystyle\geq{\mu(\lambda)}-2\,{\mathrm{negl}(\lambda)}

\displaystyle\text{(\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{eq:ci_equiv_assumption})},

which implies
3\,{\mathrm{negl}(\lambda)}\geq{\mu(\lambda)}
, a contradiction. Hence we have proved
Eq.

4.179
.

Now, for any
\theta\in[2N]\cup\{0,\diamond\}
, we have

\sum_{v}\tau^{\theta,v}\otimes\alpha^{\theta,v}\overset{c}{\approx}_{\epsilon+{\mathrm{negl}(\lambda)}}\mathcal{V}\sigma^{\theta}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}\overset{c}{\approx}_{\mathrm{negl}(\lambda)}\mathcal{V}\sigma^{0}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}\overset{c}{\approx}_{\epsilon+{\mathrm{negl}(\lambda)}}\sum_{v}\tau^{0,v}\otimes\alpha^{\diamond}/2^{2N}=\sum_{v}\tau^{\theta,v}\otimes\alpha^{\diamond}/2^{2N},

(4.184)

where we used
Eq.

4.178
in the first approximation, the computational indistinguishability of the
\sigma^{\theta}
s (
Lemma

4.11
) in the second approximation,
Eq.

4.179
in the third approximation, and the fact that
\sum_{v}\tau^{\theta,v}=\mathbbm{1}
for all
\theta\in[2N]\cup\{0,\diamond\}
in the last equality.

Setting
\alpha\coloneqq\alpha^{\diamond}
,
the lemma follows from
Eq.

4.184
, the triangle inequality for computational indistinguishability (
Lemma

2.13
), and noting that
the computational distinguishability between

\sum_{v}\tau^{\theta,v}\otimes\alpha^{\theta,v}

and
\sum_{v}\tau^{\theta,v}\otimes\alpha^{\diamond}/2^{2N}
can be expressed as the sum-over-
v
of the computational distinguishabilities between
\alpha^{\theta,v}
and
\alpha^{\diamond}/2^{2N}
, which we set as
\delta(v)
.
∎

4.9
Soundness for states and measurements

We now put everything together to give our main theorem,
4.40
.

First, we need to do some bookkeeping. In most of the preceding analysis, we have considered the device as an efficient
perfect
device in the knowledge that the states
\psi^{\theta}
of the actual device are equal to those of a perfect device up to error
\sqrt{\gamma_{P}}
in trace distance, see
Proposition

4.16
. In fact,
\gamma_{P}
also controls how close the states
\sigma^{\theta,v}
of the actual device are to those of a perfect device:

Lemma 4.39
.

Let
D
be an efficient device and
\widetilde{D}
the perfect device associated with
D
according to
Proposition

4.16
. Respectively, let
\sigma^{\theta,v},\tilde{\sigma}^{\theta,v}\in\mathrm{Pos}(\mathcal{H})
be the states prepared by
D
and
\widetilde{D}
in the Hadamard round as defined in
4.4
. Then, for all
\theta\in[2N]\cup\{0,\diamond\}
, we have

\sum_{v\in\{0,1\}^{2N}}\|\sigma^{\theta,v}-\tilde{\sigma}^{\theta,v}\|_{1}\leq\sqrt{\gamma_{P}}+2\gamma_{P}.

(4.185)

Proof.

Since
\sigma^{\theta}
is obtained from
\psi^{\theta}
by a quantum channel, which cannot increase trace distance,
Proposition

4.16
implies
\|\sigma^{\theta}-\tilde{\sigma}^{\theta}\|_{1}\leq\sqrt{\gamma_{P}}
. As the operators in
\{\sigma^{\theta,v}-\tilde{\sigma}^{\theta,v}|v\in\{0,1\}^{2N}\}
are Hermitian and pairwise multiply to
0
by
4.4
, we have

\sum_{v\in\{0,1\}^{2N}}\|\sigma^{\theta,v}-\tilde{\sigma}^{\theta,v}\|_{1}=\Bigl\lVert\sum_{v\in\{0,1\}^{2N}}(\sigma^{\theta,v}-\tilde{\sigma}^{\theta,v})\Bigr\rVert_{1}\leq\|\sigma^{\theta}-\tilde{\sigma}^{\theta}\|_{1}+2\gamma_{P}\leq\sqrt{\gamma_{P}}+2\gamma_{P},

(4.186)

where the first equality uses
Lemma

2.7
and the first inequality uses
Lemma

4.13
. Hence the lemma.
∎

To state our theorem, we recall the definition of
\tau^{\theta,v}
from
4.34
:
\tau^{\theta,v}\coloneqq|\tau^{\theta,v}\rangle\langle\tau^{\theta,v}|
, where

\ket{\tau^{\theta,v}}\coloneqq\begin{cases}\ket{v_{1}}\otimes\cdots\otimes\ket{v_{\theta-1}}\otimes\ket{(-)^{v_{\theta}}}\otimes\ket{v_{\theta+1}}\otimes\cdots\otimes\ket{v_{2N}}&\text{if $\theta\in[2N]$},\\
\ket{v}\coloneqq\ket{v_{1}}\otimes\cdots\otimes\ket{v_{2N}}&\text{if $\theta=0$},\\
\ket{\psi^{v}}&\text{if $\theta=\diamond$},\end{cases}

(4.187)

where
\ket{\psi^{v}}
is as defined in
Eq.

3.5
.

We also write, for all
u\in\{0,1\}^{2N}
, and
q\in\{0,1\}^{2N}
,
\ket{B_{q}^{u}}
for the
2N
-qubit state

\ket{B_{q}^{u}}\coloneqq\ket{B_{q_{1}}^{u_{1}}}\ket{B_{q_{2}}^{u_{2}}}\cdots\ket{B_{q_{2N}}^{u_{2N}}},

(4.188)

where
\ket{B_{q_{i}}^{u_{i}}}
denotes the BB84 states as in
Eq.

1.3
.

Note that
\Pi_{q}^{u}=|B_{q}^{u}\rangle\langle B_{q}^{u}|
for all
q\in\{0,1\}^{2N}
and
u\in\{0,1\}^{2N}
, where
\Pi_{q}^{u}
is as defined in
Eq.

1.2
. We finally stress that the theorem holds under the LWE hardness assumption made throughout this work.

Theorem 4.40
.

Let
D
be an efficient device. Let
\mathcal{H}\coloneqq\mathcal{H}_{D}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{Y}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{R}
be the Hilbert space of
D
. Let
\mathcal{V}:\mathcal{H}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}^{2^{2N}}\otimes\mathcal{H}
be the swap isometry defined in
4.30
. For
\theta\in[2N]\cup\{0,\diamond\}
and
v\in\{0,1\}^{2N}
, let
\sigma^{\theta,v}\in\mathrm{Pos}(\mathcal{H})
be the states that
D
prepares after returning the first answer in the Hadamard round, as defined in
4.4
. Let
\{\{P^{u}_{q}\}_{u\in\{0,1\}^{2N}}\,|\,q\in\{0,1\}^{2N}\}
be the measurements
D
performs to return the second answer in the Hadamard round when asked question
q
.

Suppose that
D
fails the protocol in
Fig.

2
(with an input distribution
\mu
on
\{0,1\}^{2N}
and
N=\mathrm{poly}(\lambda)
) with probability at most
\epsilon
. Then, there exist states
\{\alpha^{\theta,v}\,|\,\theta\in[2N]\cup\{0,\diamond\},v\in\{0,1\}^{2N}\}
, that are computationally indistinguishable from a single state
\alpha\in\mathrm{Pos}(\mathcal{H})
in the way specified in
Lemma

4.38
, such that

\displaystyle\sum_{v\in\{0,1\}^{2N}}\|\mathcal{V}\sigma^{\theta,v}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}-\tau^{\theta,v}\otimes\alpha^{\theta,v}\|_{1}\leq O(N^{7/4}\epsilon^{1/32}),

(4.189)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{q\leftarrow_{\mu}\{0,1\}^{2N}}\Biggl[\sum_{u,v\in\{0,1\}^{2N}}\|\mathcal{V}P_{q}^{u}\sigma^{\theta,v}P_{q}^{u}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}-\bra{B_{q}^{u}}\tau^{\theta,v}\ket{B_{q}^{u}}|B_{q}^{u}\rangle\langle B_{q}^{u}|\otimes\alpha^{\theta,v}\|_{1}\Biggr]\leq O(N^{2}\epsilon^{1/32}),

(4.190)

and, for all
q\in\{0^{2N},1^{2N},0^{N}1^{N},1^{N}0^{N}\}
,

\sum_{u,v\in\{0,1\}^{2N}}\|\mathcal{V}P_{q}^{u}\sigma^{\theta,v}P_{q}^{u}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}-\bra{B_{q}^{u}}\tau^{\theta,v}\ket{B_{q}^{u}}|B_{q}^{u}\rangle\langle B_{q}^{u}|\otimes\alpha^{\theta,v}\|_{1}\leq O(N^{2}\epsilon^{1/32}).

(4.191)

Proof.

Let
\widetilde{D}
be the perfect device associated with
D
according to
Proposition

4.16
. Let
\tilde{\sigma}^{\theta,v}
be the states of
\widetilde{D}
corresponding to
\sigma^{\theta,v}
of
D
.

We first prove
Eq.

4.189
, which characterizes the pre-measurement states
\sigma^{\theta,v}
. We have

\displaystyle\sum_{v}\|\mathcal{V}\sigma^{\theta,v}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}-\tau^{\theta,v}\otimes\alpha^{\theta,v}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{v}\|\mathcal{V}\sigma^{\theta,v}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}-\mathcal{V}\tilde{\sigma}^{\theta,v}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}\|_{1}+\sum_{v}\|\mathcal{V}\tilde{\sigma}^{\theta,v}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}-\tau^{\theta,v}\otimes\alpha^{\theta,v}\|_{1}

(4.192)

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\gamma_{P}}+2\gamma_{P}+O(N^{17/16}\gamma_{H}^{1/32}+N^{3/2}\gamma_{H}^{1/4}),

where, in the last inequality, we used
Lemma

4.39
to bound the first term and took the sum of the bounds in
Lemmas

4.35
and
4.37
to bound the second term in a way that is independent of
\theta
.

To get a bound in terms of
N
and the failure probability,
\epsilon
, we can use
Proposition

4.7
, which says
\gamma_{P},\gamma_{H}=O(N\epsilon)
. Therefore, we obtain

\sum_{v}\|\mathcal{V}\sigma^{\theta,v}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}-\tau^{\theta,v}\otimes\alpha^{\theta,v}\|_{1}\leq O(N^{7/4}\epsilon^{1/32}).

(4.193)

Hence the first equation of the theorem,
Eq.

4.189
.

We now proceed to prove the second and third equations of the theorem,
Eqs.

4.190
and
4.191
, which characterize the post-measurement states
P^{u}_{q}\sigma^{\theta,v}P^{u}_{q}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}
. We have

\displaystyle\sum_{u,v}\|\mathcal{V}P^{u}_{q}\sigma^{\theta,v}P^{u}_{q}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}-\bra{B_{q}^{u}}\tau^{\theta,v}\ket{B_{q}^{u}}|B_{q}^{u}\rangle\langle B_{q}^{u}|\otimes\alpha^{\theta,v}\|_{1}

(4.194)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{u,v}\|\mathcal{V}P^{u}_{q}\sigma^{\theta,v}P^{u}_{q}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}-\mathcal{V}P^{u}_{q}\tilde{\sigma}^{\theta,v}P^{u}_{q}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}\|_{1}+\|\mathcal{V}P^{u}_{q}\tilde{\sigma}^{\theta,v}P^{u}_{q}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}-\bra{B_{q}^{u}}\tau^{\theta,v}\ket{B_{q}^{u}}|B_{q}^{u}\rangle\langle B_{q}^{u}|\otimes\alpha^{\theta,v}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{v}\|\sigma^{\theta,v}-\tilde{\sigma}^{\theta,v}\|_{1}+\sum_{u,v}\|\mathcal{V}P^{u}_{q}\tilde{\sigma}^{\theta,v}P^{u}_{q}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}-\bra{B_{q}^{u}}\tau^{\theta,v}\ket{B_{q}^{u}}|B_{q}^{u}\rangle\langle B_{q}^{u}|\otimes\alpha^{\theta,v}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\gamma_{P}}+2\gamma_{P}+\sum_{u,v}\|\mathcal{V}P^{u}_{q}\tilde{\sigma}^{\theta,v}P^{u}_{q}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}-\bra{B_{q}^{u}}\tau^{\theta,v}\ket{B_{q}^{u}}|B_{q}^{u}\rangle\langle B_{q}^{u}|\otimes\alpha^{\theta,v}\|_{1},

where the second inequality uses part
4
of the replacement lemma (
Lemma

2.6
) and the third inequality uses
Lemma

4.39
.

Equation

4.194
allows us to assume that
D
is already perfect and add on
\sqrt{\gamma_{P}}+2\gamma_{P}
to the bound we derive on the first expression in
Eq.

4.194
only at the end. Henceforth, we assume
D
is perfect until indicated otherwise.

To prove the second part of the theorem, it primarily remains to prove

\sum_{u,v}\|\mathcal{V}P_{q}^{u}\sigma^{\theta,v}P_{q}^{u}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}-\bra{B_{q}^{u}}\tau^{\theta,v}\ket{B_{q}^{u}}|B_{q}^{u}\rangle\langle B_{q}^{u}|\otimes\alpha^{\theta,v}\|_{1}\leq O(N^{3/2}\gamma_{H}^{1/32}+\sqrt{N}\gamma_{H,q}^{1/8}),

(4.195)

for all
q\in\{0,1\}^{2N}
. In the following, we prove
Eq.

4.195
for
q=1^{2N}
. After the proof, we explain how this proof can be slightly modified to prove
Eq.

4.195
for
q\neq 1^{2N}
.

Consider
Eq.

4.195
for
q=1^{2N}
. For
u\in\{0,1\}^{2N}
, we have

P_{1^{2N}}^{u}=\prod_{i\in[2N]}X_{i}^{(u_{i})}.

(4.196)

Now,
Lemma

4.32
and
Lemma

2.10
gives: for all
i\in[2N]
and
\theta\in[2N]\cup\{0,\diamond\}
,
\mathcal{V}X_{i}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}\approx_{\delta,\mathcal{V}\sigma^{\theta}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}}\sigma^{X}_{i}\otimes\mathbbm{1}
, where

\delta\coloneqq(N\sqrt{\gamma_{H}})^{1/2}.

(4.197)

Therefore, by
Lemma

2.11
, we have

\mathcal{V}X_{i}^{(u_{i})}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}\approx_{\delta,\mathcal{V}\sigma^{\theta}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}}|(-)^{u_{i}}\rangle\langle(-)^{u_{i}}|_{i}\otimes\mathbbm{1}.

(4.198)

We write

\displaystyle X_{j}[u]\coloneqq X_{2N}^{(u_{2N})}X_{2N-1}^{(u_{2N-1})}\cdots X_{j}^{(u_{j})}=X_{j}^{(u_{j})}X_{j+1}^{(u_{j+1})}\cdots X_{2N}^{(u_{2N})},

(4.199)

\displaystyle|(-)^{u}\rangle\langle(-)^{u}|\coloneqq|(-)^{u_{1}}\rangle\langle(-)^{u_{1}}|_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes|(-)^{u_{2N}}\rangle\langle(-)^{u_{2N}}|_{2N},

and recall
|(-)^{u_{j:2N}}\rangle\langle(-)^{u_{j:2N}}|\coloneqq\mathbbm{1}_{2}^{\otimes(j-1)}\otimes|(-)^{u_{j}}\rangle\langle(-)^{u_{j}}|\otimes\ldots\otimes|(-)^{u_{2N}}\rangle\langle(-)^{u_{2N}}|
from
Eq.

4.156
.

Then, by direct calculation, we have

\displaystyle\lVert\mathcal{V}X_{1}[u]\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}-|(-)^{u}\rangle\langle(-)^{u}|\otimes\mathbbm{1}\rVert^{2}_{\mathcal{V}\sigma^{\theta}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}}

(4.200)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}[X_{1}[u]\sigma^{\theta}]+\mathrm{Tr}[(|(-)^{u}\rangle\langle(-)^{u}|\otimes\mathbbm{1})\mathcal{V}\sigma^{\theta}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}]-2\real\mathrm{Tr}[(|(-)^{u}\rangle\langle(-)^{u}|\otimes\mathbbm{1})\mathcal{V}X_{1}[u]\sigma^{\theta}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}].

We proceed to show that
Eq.

4.200
summed over
u
is close to zero. We do this by showing that the vector

\sum_{u}\real\mathrm{Tr}[(|(-)^{u}\rangle\langle(-)^{u}|\otimes\mathbbm{1})\mathcal{V}X_{1}[u]\sigma^{\theta}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}]\ket{u}

(4.201)

is approximately equal to both

\sum_{u}\mathrm{Tr}[X_{1}[u]\sigma^{\theta}]\ket{u}\quad\text{and}\quad\sum_{u}\mathrm{Tr}[(|(-)^{u}\rangle\langle(-)^{u}|\otimes\mathbbm{1})\mathcal{V}\sigma^{\theta}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}]\ket{u}

(4.202)

in
\ell_{1}
-norm distance.

In the following, we recall that, for vectors
x,y\in\mathbb{C}^{n}
, we defined
x\approx_{\epsilon}y
to mean
\|x-y\|_{1}\leq O(\epsilon)
, where
\|\cdot\|_{1}
denotes the
\ell_{1}
-norm. We give justifications for the steps below that do not involve an exact equality afterwards. The exact equalities follow from
\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}\mathcal{V}=\mathbbm{1}
,
X_{1}[u]=X_{2}[u]X_{1}^{(u_{1})}
,
|(-)^{u_{1}}\rangle\langle(-)^{u_{1}}|_{1}\cdot|(-)^{u}\rangle\langle(-)^{u}|=|(-)^{u_{1}}\rangle\langle(-)^{u_{1}}|_{1}\cdot|(-)^{u_{2:2N}}\rangle\langle(-)^{u_{2:2N}}|
, and the cyclicity of the trace.

\displaystyle\sum_{u}\real\mathrm{Tr}[(|(-)^{u}\rangle\langle(-)^{u}|\otimes\mathbbm{1})\mathcal{V}X_{1}[u]\sigma^{\theta}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}]\ket{u}

(4.203)

\displaystyle=\sum_{u}\real\mathrm{Tr}[(|(-)^{u}\rangle\langle(-)^{u}|\otimes\mathbbm{1})(\mathcal{V}X_{2}[u]X_{1}^{(u_{1})}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger})(\mathcal{V}\sigma^{\theta}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger})]\ket{u}

\displaystyle\simeq_{{\mathrm{negl}(\lambda)}}\sum_{u}\real\mathrm{Tr}[(|(-)^{u}\rangle\langle(-)^{u}|\otimes\mathbbm{1})(\mathcal{V}X_{2}[u]X_{1}^{(u_{1})}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger})(\mathcal{V}\sigma^{1}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger})]\ket{u}

\displaystyle\approx_{\sqrt{\gamma_{H}}}\sum_{u}\real\mathrm{Tr}[(|(-)^{u}\rangle\langle(-)^{u}|\otimes\mathbbm{1})(\mathcal{V}X_{2}[u]\sigma^{1}X_{1}^{(u_{1})}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger})]\ket{u}

\displaystyle=\sum_{u}\real\mathrm{Tr}[(\mathcal{V}X_{1}^{(u_{1})}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger})(|(-)^{u}\rangle\langle(-)^{u}|\otimes\mathbbm{1})(\mathcal{V}X_{2}[u]\mathcal{V}^{\dagger})(\mathcal{V}\sigma^{1}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger})]\ket{u}

\displaystyle\approx_{\sqrt{\delta}}\sum_{u}\real\mathrm{Tr}[(|(-)^{u_{1}}\rangle\langle(-)^{u_{1}}|_{1}\otimes\mathbbm{1})(|(-)^{u}\rangle\langle(-)^{u}|\otimes\mathbbm{1})(\mathcal{V}X_{2}[u]\mathcal{V}^{\dagger})(\mathcal{V}\sigma^{1}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger})]\ket{u}

\displaystyle=\sum_{u}\real\mathrm{Tr}[(|(-)^{u_{1}}\rangle\langle(-)^{u_{1}}|_{1}\otimes\mathbbm{1})(|(-)^{u_{2:2N}}\rangle\langle(-)^{u_{2:2N}}|\otimes\mathbbm{1})(\mathcal{V}X_{2}[u]\mathcal{V}^{\dagger})(\mathcal{V}\sigma^{1}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger})]\ket{u}

\displaystyle\simeq_{{\mathrm{negl}(\lambda)}}\sum_{u}\real\mathrm{Tr}[(|(-)^{u_{1}}\rangle\langle(-)^{u_{1}}|_{1}\otimes\mathbbm{1})(|(-)^{u_{2:2N}}\rangle\langle(-)^{u_{2:2N}}|\otimes\mathbbm{1})(\mathcal{V}X_{2}[u]\mathcal{V}^{\dagger})(\mathcal{V}\sigma^{\theta}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger})]\ket{u}

\displaystyle=\sum_{u}\real\mathrm{Tr}[(|(-)^{u}\rangle\langle(-)^{u}|\otimes\mathbbm{1})\mathcal{V}X_{2}[u]\sigma^{\theta}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}]\ket{u}.

We justify the four steps above that do not involve an exact equality as follows:

1.

In the third line, we used the “moreover” part of the lifting-under-projections lemma (
Lemma

2.17
) with

Q^{w}=\mathbbm{1}
,
\Pi^{u}=|(-)^{u}\rangle\langle(-)^{u}|\otimes\mathbbm{1}
,

P^{u}=\mathcal{V}X_{1}[u]\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}
,
\psi=\mathcal{V}\sigma^{\theta}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}
,
and
\psi^{\prime}=\mathcal{V}\sigma^{1}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}
.

2.

In the fourth line, we used part
4
of the replacement lemma (
Lemma

2.6
) with
P^{u}=|(-)^{u}\rangle\langle(-)^{u}|\otimes\mathbbm{1}
,

\displaystyle\psi=\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{V}(X_{2}[u]X_{1}^{(u_{1})}\sigma^{1}+\sigma^{1}X_{1}^{(u_{1})}X_{2}[u])\mathcal{V}^{\dagger},\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle\psi^{\prime}=\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{V}(X_{2}[u]\sigma^{1}X_{1}^{(u_{1})}+X_{1}^{(u_{1})}\sigma^{1}X_{2}[u])\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}.

Note that
\|\psi-\psi^{\prime}\|_{1}\leq O(\sqrt{\gamma_{H}})
by
\|\mathcal{V}\|_{\infty}=\|X_{2}[u]\|_{\infty}=1
, and the operator-state commutation relation (
Proposition

4.29
).

3.

In the sixth line, we used part
3
of the replacement lemma (
Lemma

2.6
) with

A=\mathcal{V}X_{1}^{(u_{1})}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}
,
B=|(-)^{u_{1}}\rangle\langle(-)^{u_{1}}|_{1}\otimes\mathbbm{1}
,
X_{i}^{(u_{i})}=|(-)^{u_{i}}\rangle\langle(-)^{u_{i}}|_{i}\otimes\mathbbm{1}
for
i\in[2N]
,

Y_{i}=\mathcal{V}X_{i}^{(u_{i})}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}
for
i\in\{2,\ldots,2N\}
,
and
\psi=\mathcal{V}\sigma^{1}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}
. Importantly, we used
A\approx_{\delta,\psi}B
from
Eq.

4.198
.

4.

In the second-to-last line, we again used the “moreover” part of the lifting-under-projections lemma (
Lemma

2.17
), this time with
Q^{u_{1}}=|(-)^{u_{1}}\rangle\langle(-)^{u_{1}}|_{1}\otimes\mathbbm{1}
,
\Pi^{u}=|(-)^{u_{2:2N}}\rangle\langle(-)^{u_{2:2N}}|\otimes\mathbbm{1}
,
P^{u}=\mathcal{V}X_{2}[u]\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}
,
\psi=\mathcal{V}\sigma^{1}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}
,
and
\psi^{\prime}=\mathcal{V}\sigma^{\theta}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}
.

Comparing the expressions in the first and last lines (of the equations starting at
Eq.

4.203
), we see that we can continue in the same way another
2N-1
times, such that
\sigma^{\theta}
is replaced by
\sigma^{k}
by the lifting-under-projections lemma (
Lemma

2.17
) at time
k
, to obtain

\sum_{u}\real\mathrm{Tr}[(|(-)^{u}\rangle\langle(-)^{u}|\otimes\mathbbm{1})\mathcal{V}X_{1}[u]\sigma^{\theta}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}]\ket{u}\approx_{\delta^{\prime}}\sum_{u}\real\mathrm{Tr}[(|(-)^{u}\rangle\langle(-)^{u}|\otimes\mathbbm{1})\mathcal{V}\sigma^{\theta}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}]\ket{u},

(4.204)

where

\delta^{\prime}\coloneqq N(\sqrt{\delta}+\sqrt{\gamma_{H}}+{\mathrm{negl}(\lambda)})\leq O(N^{5/4}\gamma_{H}^{1/8}).

(4.205)

But
\real\mathrm{Tr}[(|(-)^{u}\rangle\langle(-)^{u}|\otimes\mathbbm{1})\mathcal{V}\sigma^{\theta}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}]=\mathrm{Tr}[(|(-)^{u}\rangle\langle(-)^{u}|\otimes\mathbbm{1})\mathcal{V}\sigma^{\theta}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}](\geq 0)
.
Therefore,

\sum_{u}\real\mathrm{Tr}[(|(-)^{u}\rangle\langle(-)^{u}|\otimes\mathbbm{1})\mathcal{V}X_{1}[u]\sigma^{\theta}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}]\ket{u}\approx_{\delta^{\prime}}\sum_{u}\mathrm{Tr}[(|(-)^{u}\rangle\langle(-)^{u}|\otimes\mathbbm{1})\mathcal{V}\sigma^{\theta}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}]\ket{u}.

(4.206)

By an analogous argument, we can “merge”
|(-)^{u}\rangle\langle(-)^{u}|
into
\mathcal{V}X_{1}[u]\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}
(instead of merging
\mathcal{V}X_{1}[u]\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}
into
|(-)^{u}\rangle\langle(-)^{u}|
as done above) to obtain

\sum_{u}\real\mathrm{Tr}[(|(-)^{u}\rangle\langle(-)^{u}|\otimes\mathbbm{1})\mathcal{V}X_{1}[u]\sigma^{\theta}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}]\ket{u}\approx_{\delta^{\prime}}\sum_{u}\mathrm{Tr}[X_{1}[u]\sigma^{\theta}]\ket{u}.

(4.207)

Substituting
Eqs.

4.206
and
4.207
into
Eq.

4.200
gives

\sum_{u}\lVert\mathcal{V}X_{1}[u]\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}-|(-)^{u}\rangle\langle(-)^{u}|\otimes\mathbbm{1}\rVert^{2}_{\mathcal{V}\sigma^{\theta}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}}\leq O(\delta^{\prime}).

(4.208)

Then, applying
Lemma

2.5
to
Eq.

4.208
gives

\sum_{u,v}\lVert\mathcal{V}X_{1}[u]\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}-|(-)^{u}\rangle\langle(-)^{u}|\otimes\mathbbm{1}\rVert^{2}_{\mathcal{V}\sigma^{\theta,v}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}}\leq O(\delta^{\prime}).

(4.209)

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\quad\sum_{u,v}\mathcal{V}P_{1^{2N}}^{u}\sigma^{\theta,v}P_{1^{2N}}^{u}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}\otimes|u,v\rangle\langle u,v|

\displaystyle=\sum_{u,v}(\mathcal{V}X_{1}[u]\mathcal{V}^{\dagger})(\mathcal{V}\sigma^{\theta,v}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger})(\mathcal{V}X_{1}[u]\mathcal{V}^{\dagger})\otimes|u,v\rangle\langle u,v|

(
Eq.

4.196
and
\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}\mathcal{V}=\mathbbm{1}
)

\displaystyle\approx_{\delta^{\prime}}\sum_{u,v}(|(-)^{u}\rangle\langle(-)^{u}|\otimes\mathbbm{1})\mathcal{V}\sigma^{\theta,v}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}(|(-)^{u}\rangle\langle(-)^{u}|\otimes\mathbbm{1})\otimes|u,v\rangle\langle u,v|

\displaystyle\hskip-3.0pt\begin{pmatrix}\text{Part $1$ of \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{lem:post_meas_approx}}\\
\text{with \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{eq:proj_diff_bound}}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle\approx_{\delta^{\prime\prime 2}}\sum_{u,v}(|(-)^{u}\rangle\langle(-)^{u}|\otimes\mathbbm{1})\tau^{\theta,v}\otimes\alpha^{\theta,v}(|(-)^{u}\rangle\langle(-)^{u}|\otimes\mathbbm{1})\otimes|u,v\rangle\langle u,v|

\displaystyle\hskip-3.0pt\begin{pmatrix}\text{Part $2$ of \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{lem:post_meas_approx}}\\
\text{with \lx@cref{creftypeplural\lx@tilde refnum}{lem:swap_states_nondiamond} and\lx@nobreakspace\lx@cref{refnum}{lem:swap_states_diamond}}\end{pmatrix},

where

\delta^{\prime\prime}\coloneqq N^{3/2}\gamma_{H}^{1/4}+N^{17/16}\gamma_{H}^{1/32}.

(4.210)

Note that the above approximations are for operators and we recall that for two operators
A
and
B
, we defined
A\approx_{\epsilon}B
to mean
\|A-B\|_{1}^{2}\leq O(\epsilon)
, where we stress the square on the Schatten
1
-norm. Therefore, also recalling
\ket{B_{u,1^{2N}}}\coloneqq\ket{(-)^{u}}
, we can use the triangle inequality to obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{u,v}\|\mathcal{V}P_{1^{2N}}^{u}\sigma^{\theta,v}P_{1^{2N}}^{u}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}-\bra{B_{1^{2N}}^{u}}\tau^{\theta,v}\ket{B_{1^{2N}}^{u}}|B_{1^{2N}}^{u}\rangle\langle B_{1^{2N}}^{u}|\otimes\alpha^{\theta,v}\|_{1}

(4.211)

\displaystyle\leq O(\sqrt{\delta^{\prime}}+\delta^{\prime\prime})\leq O(N^{5/8}\gamma_{H}^{1/16}+N^{3/2}\gamma_{H}^{1/4}+N^{17/16}\gamma_{H}^{1/32})\leq O(N^{3/2}\gamma_{H}^{1/32}),

where we substituted the definitions of
\delta^{\prime}
and
\delta^{\prime\prime}
, given in
Eqs.

4.205
and
4.210
respectively, in the second inequality. This completes the proof of
Eq.

4.195
for
q=1^{2N}
.

We now explain how the above proof of
Eq.

4.195
for
q=1^{2N}
can be slightly modified to prove
Eq.

4.195
for
q\neq 1^{2N}
. When
q\neq 1^{2N}
, the third and second-to-last lines of the equations starting at
Eq.

4.203
require slight modification: to commute
Z_{q,i}^{(u_{i})}
past
\sigma^{i}
(instead of moving
X_{1}^{(u_{1})}
past
\sigma^{\theta}
as done above), we need to use the computational indistinguishability of
\sigma^{i}
with
\sigma^{j}
for some
j\neq i
. This is due to the conditions in the operator-state commutation lemma,
Proposition

4.29
. In addition, we need to redefine the terms
\delta
and
\delta^{\prime}
appearing in the proof of
Eq.

4.195
for
q=1^{2N}
by

\delta\coloneqq(N\sqrt{\gamma_{H}}+\gamma_{H,q}+{\mathrm{negl}(\lambda)})^{1/2}\quad\text{and}\quad\delta^{\prime}\coloneqq N(\sqrt{\delta}+\sqrt{\gamma_{H}+\gamma_{H,q}}+{\mathrm{negl}(\lambda)}),

(4.212)

while
\delta^{\prime\prime}
can be left unchanged. These re-definitions are simply due to
Lemma

4.32
and
Proposition

4.29
giving different results for different
Z_{q,i}
and
X_{q,i}
. With these modifications in place, we can use the first inequality of
Eq.

4.211
to obtain
Eq.

4.195
for
q\neq 1^{2N}
.

At this point, we drop the assumption that
D
is perfect. In this case, as explained at the start of proof of
Eq.

4.190
, we obtain the bound

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\sum_{u,v}\|\mathcal{V}P_{q}^{u}\sigma^{\theta,v}P_{q}^{u}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}-\bra{B_{q}^{u}}\tau^{\theta,v}\ket{B_{q}^{u}}|B_{q}^{u}\rangle\langle B_{q}^{u}|\otimes\alpha^{\theta,v}\|_{1}

(4.213)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{\gamma_{P}}+2\gamma_{P}+O(N^{2}\epsilon^{1/32}+N\epsilon_{H,q}^{1/8})\leq O(\sqrt{N\epsilon}+N^{49/32}\epsilon^{1/32}+N^{5/8}\epsilon_{H,q}^{1/8})\leq O(N^{2}\epsilon^{1/32}+N\epsilon_{H,q}^{1/8}),

where we also used
Eq.

4.195
for the first inequality and
Proposition

4.7
for the second inequality.

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\quad\mathbb{E}_{q\leftarrow_{\mu}\{0,1\}^{2N}}\Biggl[\sum_{u,v\in\{0,1\}^{2N}}\|\mathcal{V}P_{q}^{u}\sigma^{\theta,v}P_{q}^{u}-\bra{B_{q}^{u}}\tau^{\theta,v}\ket{B_{q}^{u}}|B_{q}^{u}\rangle\langle B_{q}^{u}|\otimes\alpha^{\theta,v}\|_{1}\Biggr]

\displaystyle\leq O\Bigl(\sum_{q\in\{0,1\}^{2N}}\mu(q)\bigl(N^{2}\epsilon^{1/32}+N\epsilon_{H,q}^{1/8}\bigr)\Bigr)

(
Eq.

4.213
)

\displaystyle\leq O\Bigl(N^{2}\epsilon^{1/32}+N\Bigl(\sum_{q\in\{0,1\}^{2N}}\mu(q)\epsilon_{H,q}\Bigr)^{1/8}\Bigr)

(Jensen’s inequality)

\displaystyle\leq O(N^{2}\epsilon^{1/32})

\displaystyle\text{(\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{def:failure_prob})}.

Therefore, we have proved the second equation of the theorem,
Eq.

4.190
. The last equation of the theorem,
Eq.

4.191
, follows by applying
4.6
to
Eq.

4.213
.
∎

5
Applications

5.1
Device-independent quantum key distribution

In this section, we describe how to adapt the protocol for DIQKD under computational assumptions in
[
MDCAF21
]
to use our self-testing protocol as its main component. The resulting DIQKD protocol operates under the same setting and assumptions as in
[
MDCAF21
]
except we remove the IID assumption. In particular, we highlight the fact that we retain the advantage of the generated key being information-theoretically secure. For details about the setting and assumptions, we refer the reader to
[
MDCAF21
]
, in particular, its Figure 1. Our protocol will also make use of a “cut-and-choose” technique in
[
GMP23
, Theorem 4.33 (arXiv version v2)]
). Henceforth, DIQKD without qualification refers to DIQKD under computational assumptions.

Recall that in our self-testing protocol there is a single verifier interacting with a single device. On the other hand, in DIQKD, there are two verifiers, Alice and Bob, that each interact with their own (untrusted) device. In DIQKD
under computational assumptions
, the two devices are not assumed to be non-communicating and are modeled as a single device with two
components
, one on Alice’s side, and one on Bob’s. At a high level, to resolve the difference in the number of verifiers, we will let Alice play the role of the single verifier in our self-testing protocol while Bob will play a relaying role.

In
Fig.

5
, we describe a single test round of our DIQKD protocol. In
Fig.

6
, we describe how to modify the test round to give a single generation round of our DIQKD protocol. Note that all communication between Alice and Bob are via their public authenticated channel. Eve, who is computationally unbounded, may compute any secret information hidden in this communication but we make the standard assumption that she cannot send the secret information to the devices (e.g., see
[
MDCAF21
, Figure 1]
).

1.

Alice samples
\theta\leftarrow_{U}[2N]\cup\{0,\diamond\}
uniformly at randomly, generates
2N
key-trapdoor pairs
(k_{1},t_{1})
,
\dots
,
(k_{2N},t_{2N})
according to
\theta
, and sends
k_{N+1},\dots,k_{2N}
to Bob. Note that Alice has all the trapdoors
\{t_{i}\}_{i=1}^{2N}
. Then Alice sends
k_{1},\dots,k_{N}
to her component. Bob sends
k_{N+1},\dots,k_{2N}
to his component.

2.

Alice receives back
(y_{1},\dots,y_{N})\in\mathcal{Y}^{N}
and Bob receives back images
(y_{N+1},\dots,y_{2N})\in\mathcal{Y}^{N}
.

3.

Alice samples
c\leftarrow_{U}\{\text{preimage},\text{Hadamard}\}
uniformly at random, sends it to Bob, and they both send
c
to their components.

Case
c=\text{preimage}
. Alice receives
(b_{1},\dots b_{N},x_{1},\dots,x_{N})\in\{0,1\}^{N+Nw}
from her component and Bob receives
(b_{N+1},\dots,b_{2N},x_{N+1},\dots,x_{2N})\in\{0,1\}^{N+Nw}
from his component and sends it to Alice. Alice verifies
(b_{1},\dots,b_{2N},x_{1},\dots,x_{2N})
according to our self-testing protocol.

Case
c=\text{Hadamard}
.

(a)

Alice receives
(d_{1},\dots,d_{N})\in\{0,1\}^{Nw}
from her component and Bob receives
(d_{N+1},\dots,d_{2N})\in\{0,1\}^{Nw}
from his component.

(b)

Alice samples
a\leftarrow_{U}\{0,1\}
uniformly at random.

•

If
a=0
, Alice samples
q\leftarrow_{U}\{0^{2N},1^{2N},0^{N}1^{N},1^{N}0^{N}\}
uniformly at random.

•

If
a=1
, Alice sets
q=1^{N}0^{N}
.

Note that the resulting distribution on
(q_{1},\dots,q_{2N})\in\{0,1\}^{2N}
is the same as in Step 4 of our self-testing protocol (
Fig.

2
) with
\mu
chosen as the distribution that always outputs
1^{N}0^{N}
.

Alice sends
q_{N+1}
to Bob. Alice sends
q_{1},\dots,q_{N}
to her component. Bob sends
q_{N+1},\dots,q_{N+1}
(
=q_{N+1},\dots,q_{2N}
) to his component.

(c)

Alice receives
(u_{1},\dots,u_{N})\in\{0,1\}^{N}
from her component and Bob receives
(u_{N+1},\dots,u_{2N})\in\{0,1\}^{N}
from his component. Alice sends “Test” to Bob. Bob sends
\{(y_{i},d_{i},u_{i})\}_{i=N+1}^{2N}
to Alice. Alice verifies
\{(y_{i},d_{i},u_{i})\}_{i=1}^{2N}
according to our self-testing protocol using the trapdoors that she holds,
(t_{1},\dots,t_{2N})
.

Figure 5:
Test round for device-independent quantum key distribution (DIQKD) protocol.

Same as the test round (see
Fig.

5
) except with the following modifications.

•

At Step 1, Alice chooses
\theta=\diamond
.

•

At the start of Step 3, Alice chooses
c=\text{Hadamard}
.

•

At the start of Step 3(b), instead of sampling
q
, Alice sets
q=1^{N}0^{N}
.

•

Replace Step 3 (c) by the following. Alice receives
(u_{1},\dots,u_{N})\in\{0,1\}^{N}
from her component and Bob receives
(u_{N+1},\dots,u_{2N})\in\{0,1\}^{N}
from his component. Alice sends “Generation” to Bob.

Figure 6:
Generation round for device-independent quantum key distribution (DIQKD) protocol.

We construct our overall DIQKD protocol by using multiple test rounds followed by a single generation round. More specifically, at the start of the protocol, Alice selects
m=\mathrm{poly}_{1}(N)\in\mathbb{N}
and
0<\delta=1/\mathrm{poly}_{2}(N)
, where the
\mathrm{poly}_{i}(N)
’s are some polynomials in
N
that are sufficiently large relative to the robustness bounds in
4.40
(see
[
GMP23
, Proof of Corollary 4.35 (arXiv version v2)]
). Then, Alice samples
s\leftarrow_{U}\{0,\dots,m-1\}
and performs
sm
test rounds. Let
B_{j}\coloneqq\{(j-1)m+1,\dots,jm\}
for
j=1,\dots,s
. If there exists some
j\in[s]
such that the fraction of test rounds in
B_{j}
that fail is
>\delta
, then Alice aborts the protocol. Otherwise, Alice samples
r\leftarrow[m]
and performs a further
r-1
test rounds followed by a generation round. These steps are due to our use of the aforementioned “cut-and-choose” technique in
[
GMP23
]
.

After the generation round, Alice and Bob proceed to key extraction, which is essentially the same as that in
[
MDCAF21
, Protocol 3]
. More specifically, Alice calculates
\hat{h}_{1},\dots,\hat{h}_{N}
as defined above
Eq.

3.4
. Then
Alice sets
\tilde{u}_{i}=u_{i}\oplus\hat{h}_{i}
and Bob sets
\tilde{u}_{N+i}=u_{N+i}
.
Then
(\tilde{u}_{i})_{i\in[N]}
and
(\tilde{u}_{N+i})_{i\in[N]}
are the raw shared secret keys of Alice and Bob, respectively. Finally, Alice and Bob apply one-way error correction and privacy amplification to these raw keys to obtain their final shared secret keys. This completes our description of the protocol.

The completeness and soundness of this DIQKD protocol essentially follow from the completeness and soundness of our self-testing protocol combined with the proofs of
[
MDCAF21
, Theorem 1]
and
[
GMP23
, Theorem 4.33 (arXiv version v2)]
. We sketch the salient aspects below.

Completeness (sketch).

Consider the case when the device is honest. By construction, an honest device can pass the test rounds with
\geq 1-{\mathrm{negl}(\lambda)}
probability due to the completeness of our self-testing protocol (
3.1
.) It is also clear from
Eq.

3.6
(in the case
\theta=\diamond
) that
\tilde{u}_{i}=\tilde{u}_{N+i}
for all
i\in I
. The key rate
7
7

7

In defence of our key rate, we note here that the first protocol for DIQKD in the nonlocal setting without IID assumptions
[
RUV13
]
also had a key rate that tends to zero as
N\rightarrow\infty
.
of our protocol will be
1/\mathrm{poly}(N)
because Alice can implement up to
m^{2}=\mathrm{poly}_{1}(N)^{2}
test rounds before a generation round and
\mathrm{poly}_{1}(N)
is a high-degree polynomial in
N
.

We mention that our DIQKD protocol as described requires an honest device to use nonlocal controlled-
Z
operations that act on both of its components. As discussed in
[
MDCAF21
]
, it is preferable for the honest device to be able to only use only local operations, i.e., operations that act on only one of its components. However, as pointed out in
[
MDCAF21
]
, it is straightforward to remove the need for the nonlocal controlled-
Z
operation via gate teleportation. Doing this would require some small changes to the protocol and we refer the reader to
[
MDCAF21
, Appendix A]
for details.

Soundness (sketch).

First, to prove the soundness of this protocol, we need to assume that LWE is hard even when a device has access to quantum advice, see
[
GMP23
, Remark 4.3 (arXiv version v2)]
. As pointed out in
[
GMP23
, Remark 4.3 (arXiv version v2)]
, this assumption is used in
[
GV19
,
BCM+21
,
GMP23
]
. With this assumption, the same argument in
[
GMP23
, Proof of Theorem 4.33 (arXiv version v2)]
implies that, conditioned on the protocol not aborting, the failure probability of the device in the generation round is of order
\sqrt{\delta}
.
The physical state after the generation round is

\sum_{u,v}(P_{1^{N}0^{N}}^{u})_{A^{\prime}B^{\prime}}\sigma_{A^{\prime}B^{\prime}E}^{\diamond,v}(P_{1^{N}0^{N}}^{u})_{A^{\prime}B^{\prime}}\otimes|u\rangle\langle u|_{U},

(5.1)

where
A^{\prime}
is the (quantum) register of Alice’s component of the device,
B^{\prime}
is the (quantum) register of Bob’s component of the device,
U
is the register containing Alice and Bob’s measurement outcomes, and
E
contains Eve’s quantum side information.
By
4.40
, after tracing out
systems
A^{\prime}
and
B^{\prime}
, the physical state is

\eta
-close to the ideal state

\rho=\sum_{v,u\in\{0,1\}^{2N}}\bra{B_{1^{N}0^{N}}^{u}}\tau^{\diamond,v}\ket{B_{1^{N}0^{N}}^{u}}|u\rangle\langle u|_{U}\otimes\rho_{E}^{v},

(5.2)

where
\eta\coloneqq\sqrt{\delta}+(1-\sqrt{\delta})N^{2}\delta^{1/64}=1/\mathrm{poly}(N)
, and
we recall that
\tau^{\diamond,v}
and
\ket{B_{q}^{u}}
are defined in
Eqs.

4.187
and
4.188
. After Alice and Bob changes
u_{i}
to
\tilde{u}_{i}
for
i\in[N]
and
i\in\{N+1,\dots,2N\}
, respectively, the ideal state becomes

\displaystyle\tilde{\rho}=\Bigl(\frac{1}{2^{N}}\sum_{a\in\{0,1\}^{N}}|a,a\rangle\langle a,a|_{U}\Bigr)\otimes\Bigl(\sum_{v\in\{0,1\}^{2N}}\rho_{E}^{v}\Bigr).

That is, the
register
U
becomes independent of Eve’s quantum side information, and

\displaystyle H(U\mid E)_{\tilde{\rho}}=N.

By the continuity bound of conditional entropy
[
Win16
, Lemma 2]
, the entropy in the register
U
conditioned on Eve’s side information on the physical state is at least

N-\eta N-O(\eta\log(1/\eta))\geq N-1/\mathrm{poly}(N).

(5.3)

Hence, our DIQKD protocol is capable of generating
\Omega(N)
bits of shared key.

5.2
Dimension test

In this section, we present a protocol in
Fig.

7
for testing the quantum dimension of an efficient device. This protocol is a simplified version of the self-testing protocol in
Fig.

2
.

1. Set
N=\lambda
. Sample
\theta\leftarrow_{U}\{0,1,\ldots,N\}
uniformly at random. Sample
N
key-trapdoor pairs
(k_{1},t_{k_{1}}),\ldots,(k_{N},t_{k_{N}})
from an ENTCF according to
\theta
as follows:

\theta=0
:

all pairs are sampled from
\mathrm{Gen}_{\mathcal{G}}(1^{\lambda})

\theta>0
:

the
\theta
-th key-trapdoor pair is sampled from
\mathrm{Gen}_{\mathcal{F}}(1^{\lambda})
and the remaining
N-1
pairs are all sampled from
\mathrm{Gen}_{\mathcal{G}}(1^{\lambda})
.

Send the keys
k=(k_{1},\ldots,k_{N})
to the device.

2. Receive
y=(y_{1},\ldots,y_{N})\in\mathcal{Y}^{N}
from the device.

3. Sample round type “preimage” or “Hadamard” uniformly at random and send to the device.

Case “preimage”
: receive

(b,x)=(b_{1},\ldots,b_{N},x_{1},\ldots,x_{N})

from the device, where
b\in\{0,1\}^{N}
and
x\in\{0,1\}^{Nw}
.

If
\mathrm{CHK}(k_{i},y_{i},b_{i},x_{i})=0
for all
i\in[N]
,
accept
, else
reject
.

Case “Hadamard”
:
receive

d=(d_{1},\ldots,d_{N})\in\{0,1\}^{Nw}

from the device.

Sample
q\leftarrow_{U}\{0^{N},1^{N}\}
and send
q
to the device.

Receive
u\in\{0,1\}^{N}
from the device.

1.

\theta=0
and

q=0^{N}
:

if
\hat{b}(k_{i},y_{i})\neq u_{i}
for some
i\in[N]
,
reject
, else
accept
.

q=1^{N}
:

accept
.

2.

\theta\in[N]
and

q=0^{N}
:

if
\hat{b}(k_{i},y_{i})\neq u_{i}
for some
i\neq\theta
,
reject
, else
accept
.

q=1^{N}
:

if
\hat{h}(k_{\theta},y_{\theta},d_{\theta})\neq u_{\theta}
,
reject
, else
accept
.

Figure 7:
A protocol that tests the quantum dimension of a computationally efficient device.

There exists an efficient honest quantum device that is accepted by the dimension test with probability
\geq 1-{\mathrm{negl}(\lambda)}
. The strategy of this device follows that described in the proof of
3.1
, except it is simpler because there is no need to apply controlled-
\sigma^{Z}
gates. In particular, when
q=0^{N}
, the honest device measures
N
qubits in the computational basis, and when
q=1^{N}
, it measures
N
qubits in the Hadamard basis. Moreover, there exists an efficient classical verifier, which again follows from the efficient function generation and efficient decoding properties of ENTCFs (
(a)
and
(f)
). We omit a formal completeness proof.

We first show in
5.4
that if the device can pass this protocol with high probability, it must have
N
-approximate qubits following the definitions given in
[
Vid20
]
. We reproduce these definitions below for completeness.

Definition 5.1
.

The Pauli group on
n
qubits, denoted by
\mathcal{P}_{n}
, is the multiplicative group of order
2\cdot 4^{n}
defined by

\mathcal{P}_{n}\coloneqq\{(-1)^{c}\sigma^{X}(a)\sigma^{Z}(b)\mid c\in\{0,1\},a\in\{0,1\}^{n},b\in\{0,1\}^{n}\},

(5.4)

where
\sigma^{X}(a)\coloneqq(\sigma^{X}_{1})^{a_{1}}\cdots(\sigma^{X}_{n})^{a_{n}}
and
\sigma^{Z}(b)\coloneqq(\sigma^{Z}_{1})^{b_{1}}\cdots(\sigma^{Z}_{n})^{b_{n}}
.

Definition 5.2
.

We say a Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}
has
n

\delta
-approximate qubits if there are reflections (observables that square to the identity)

X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}
and
Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{n}
on
\mathcal{H}
and a state
\rho\in\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{H})
such that
for all
a,b,a^{\prime},b^{\prime}\in\{0,1\}^{n}
,

\lVert X(a)Z(b)X(a^{\prime})Z(b^{\prime})-(-1)^{b\cdot a^{\prime}}X(a\oplus a^{\prime})Z(b\oplus b^{\prime})\rVert_{\rho}^{2}\leq\delta,

(5.5)

where
X(a)\coloneqq X_{1}^{a_{1}}\cdots X_{n}^{a_{n}}
,
Z(b)\coloneqq Z_{1}^{b_{1}}\cdots Z_{n}^{b_{n}}
, and
\oplus
denotes bit-wise XOR.

The motivation behind
5.2
is the next theorem which is a version of
[
Vid20
, Corollary 10.9]
that follows from
[
Vid20
, Theorem 10.6]
(attributed to Gowers and Hatami
[
GH17
]
).

Theorem 5.3
.

Suppose a Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}\cong\mathbb{C}^{d}
has
n

\delta
-approximate qubits with respect to reflections

X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}
and
Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{n}
on
\mathcal{H}
and a state
\rho\in\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{H})
.
Then there exists a
d^{\prime}\geq d
, an isometry
V:\mathbb{C}^{d}\to\mathbb{C}^{d^{\prime}}
, and a representation
g\colon\mathcal{P}_{n}\to\mathcal{U}(\mathbb{C}^{d^{\prime}})
such that

\displaystyle\E_{a,b}\lVert X(a)Z(b)-V^{\dagger}g(\sigma^{X}(a)\sigma^{Z}(b))V\rVert_{\rho}^{2}\leq O(\delta),

where the expectation is over uniformly random
a\leftarrow\{0,1\}^{n}
and
b\leftarrow\{0,1\}^{n}
.

Intuitively, the theorem says that the action of any product of the
X_{i}
s and
Z_{i}
s on
\rho
is approximately the same as the action of the corresponding product of the
\sigma^{X}_{i}
s and
\sigma^{Z}_{i}
s embedded in
\mathcal{H}
via
g
and
V
.
Since the
\sigma^{X}_{i}
s and
\sigma^{Z}_{i}
s specify
n
qubits in
(\mathbb{C}^{2})^{\otimes n}
, the
X_{i}
s and
Z_{i}
s specify
n

\delta
-approximate qubits in
\mathcal{H}
.

Theorem 5.4
.

Let
D
be an efficient device with Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}=\mathcal{H}_{D}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{Y}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{R}
.
If
D
passes the dimension test of
Fig.

7
with probability
\geq 1-\epsilon
, then

\mathcal{H}
has
N

\delta
-approximate qubits for

\delta\coloneqq O(N^{2}\epsilon^{1/32}).

Proof sketch.

It suffices to show that the
X_{i}
and
Z_{i}

defined in
4.3
satisfy the condition of

5.2
with
\rho
set to
\sigma^{0}
. This condition can be proved using techniques in the proof of
4.40
, in particular, those used in
Eq.

4.203
to handle products of projectors (which can also handle products of observables).
∎

While
5.3
suggests that a Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}
having
n

\delta
-approximate qubits should have a large quantum dimension, it is not apriori obvious how this can be proved. In the rest of this section, we prove that the soundness guarantee of our self-test (
4.40
) implies that any device passing the dimension test in
Fig.

7
must have a large quantum dimension.

The intuition is that when the dimension test is passed with high probability,
4.40
guarantees the existence of a quantum state
\rho^{\star}

on the quantum part of the device’s memory that is close to the maximally mixed state up to some isometry.
More specifically,
\rho^{\star}
comes from using
4.40
to force the device to perform a Hadamard basis measurement on
N
qubits that are in the computational basis and discarding the measurement results.
Then, the main proposition of this section,
Proposition

5.6
, shows that the guarantee on
\rho^{\star}
is strong enough for us to lower bound the rank of
\rho^{\star}
, which is also a lower bound on the quantum dimension of the device’s memory. We proceed to give a formal proof of soundness and stress that we are making the LWE hardness assumption throughout the rest of this section.

To prove
Proposition

5.6
, we use the vector-operator correspondence mapping,
\vc
, as defined in
[
Wat18
, Chapter 1.1.2]
. Let
A\in\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{H})
. Informally,
\vc(A)\in\mathcal{H}\otimes\mathcal{H}
is the column vector formed by concatenating the rows of
A
vertically. We will use the following properties of
\vc
without further comment:

1.

Suppose
A
is of the form
\sum_{i}\lambda_{i}|v_{i}\rangle\langle v_{i}|
with
\lambda_{i}\geq 0
. Then,
\vc(\sqrt{A})=\sum_{i}\sqrt{\lambda_{i}}\ket{v_{i}}\overline{\ket{v_{i}}}
, where the overline denotes element-wise complex conjugation.

2.

The
\ell_{2}
-norm of
\vc(A)
equals the Frobenius norm of
A
. In symbols,
\lVert\vc(A)\rVert=\lVert A\rVert_{F}
.

3.

If
B,C\in\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{H})
, then
(B\otimes C)\vc(A)=\vc(BAC^{\intercal})
, where
⊺
denotes the transpose. In particular, if
U\in\mathcal{L}(\mathcal{H})
is unitary, then
(U\otimes\overline{U})\vc(A)=\vc(UAU^{\dagger})
.

We also need the following technical lemma.

Lemma 5.5

(
[
Wat18
, Lemma 3.34]
)
.

Let
P_{1},P_{2}\in\mathrm{Pos}(\mathcal{H})
be positive semi-definite operators. Then,

\lVert\sqrt{P_{1}}-\sqrt{P_{2}}\rVert_{F}\leq\sqrt{\lVert P_{1}-P_{2}\rVert_{1}}.

(5.6)

Proposition 5.6
.

Let
\rho,\alpha\in D(\mathcal{H})
be density operators. If there exists a unitary
U\in\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{C}^{2^{n}}\otimes\mathcal{H})
such that

\|U(|0\rangle\langle 0|^{\otimes n}\otimes\rho)U^{\dagger}-2^{-n}\mathbbm{1}\otimes\alpha\|_{1}\leq\epsilon,

(5.7)

then
\Rank(\rho)\geq(1-\epsilon)2^{n}
.

Proof.

Let us write the eigen-decompositions of
\rho
and
\alpha
as

\rho=\sum_{i=1}^{N}\lambda_{i}|v_{i}\rangle\langle v_{i}|
and

\alpha=\sum_{k=1}^{N^{\prime}}\mu_{k}|u_{k}\rangle\langle u_{k}|
, where
\lambda_{i},\mu_{k}>0
. Note that
\Rank(\rho)=N
;
\sum_{i}\lambda_{i}=1
because
\rho
is normalized;
\mu_{k}\leq 1
for all
k\in[N^{\prime}]
because
\alpha
is normalized. We have

\displaystyle\vc(\sqrt{|0\rangle\langle 0|^{\otimes n}\otimes\rho})=\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sqrt{\lambda_{i}}\ket{0}^{\otimes n}\ket{v_{i}}\otimes\ket{0}^{\otimes n}\overline{\ket{v_{i}}},

(5.8)

\displaystyle\vc(\sqrt{2^{-n}\mathbbm{1}\otimes\alpha})=\sum_{k=1}^{N^{\prime}}\sum_{x\in\{0,1\}^{n}}\sqrt{\mu_{k}/2^{n}}\ket{x}\ket{u_{k}}\otimes\ket{x}\overline{\ket{u_{k}}}.

Therefore, the condition of the proposition,
Eq.

5.7
, implies that

\displaystyle\Bigl\lVert U\otimes\overline{U}\Bigl(\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sqrt{\lambda_{i}}\ket{0}^{\otimes n}\ket{v_{i}}\otimes\ket{0}^{\otimes n}\overline{\ket{v_{i}}}\Bigr)-\sum_{k=1}^{N^{\prime}}\sum_{x\in\{0,1\}^{n}}\sqrt{\mu_{k}/2^{n}}\ket{x}\ket{u_{k}}\otimes\ket{x}\overline{\ket{u_{k}}}\Bigr\rVert

(5.9)

\displaystyle=\lVert U[|0\rangle\langle 0|^{\otimes n}\otimes\sqrt{\rho}\,]U^{\dagger}-2^{-n/2}\mathbbm{1}\otimes\sqrt{\alpha}\rVert_{F}\leq\lVert U[|0\rangle\langle 0|^{\otimes n}\otimes\rho\,]U^{\dagger}-2^{-n}\mathbbm{1}\otimes\alpha\rVert_{1}^{1/2}\leq\sqrt{\epsilon}.

The key observation of this proof is the following. Let
\ket{\alpha}
,
\ket{\beta}
be bipartite states on
\mathcal{H}_{A}^{\prime}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{B}^{\prime}
such that
\ket{\alpha}
is normalized and has Schmidt rank
R
, and the Schmidt coefficients of
\ket{\beta}
are each at most
b
. Then,

\lvert\langle\alpha|\beta\rangle\rvert^{2}\leq Rb^{2}.

(5.10)

To see this, write
\ket{\alpha}=\sum_{i=1}^{R}a_{i}\ket{e_{i}}\otimes\ket{e_{i}^{\prime}}
and
\ket{\beta}=\sum_{j=1}^{d}b_{j}\ket{f_{j}}\otimes\ket{f_{j}^{\prime}}
in terms of their Schmidt decompositions, where
d\coloneqq\min\{\dim\mathcal{H},\dim\mathcal{H}^{\prime}\}
. Then, by using the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality twice,

\displaystyle\lvert\langle\alpha|\beta\rangle\rvert

\displaystyle\leq b\sum_{i=1}^{R}a_{i}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\lvert\langle e_{i}|f_{j}\rangle\rvert\lvert\langle e_{i}^{\prime}|f_{j}^{\prime}\rangle\rvert

(5.11)

\displaystyle\leq b\Bigl(\sum_{i=1}^{R}a_{i}^{2}\Bigr)^{1/2}\Bigl(\sum_{i=1}^{R}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\lvert\langle e_{i}|f_{j}\rangle\rvert\lvert\langle e_{i}^{\prime}|f_{j}^{\prime}\rangle\rvert\Bigr)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq b\Bigl(\sum_{i=1}^{R}\Bigl(\sum_{j=1}^{d}\lvert\langle e_{i}|f_{j}\rangle\rvert^{2}\Bigr)^{1/2}\Bigl(\sum_{k=1}^{d}\lvert\langle e_{i}^{\prime}|f_{k}^{\prime}\rangle\rvert^{2}\Bigr)^{1/2}\Bigr)^{1/2}=b\sqrt{R}.

To conclude the proof, we use the above observation as follows. Set

\ket{\alpha}\coloneqq U\otimes\overline{U}\Bigl(\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sqrt{\lambda_{i}}\ket{0}^{\otimes n}\ket{v_{i}}\otimes\ket{0}^{\otimes n}\overline{\ket{v_{i}}}\Bigr)\quad\text{and}\quad\ket{\beta}\coloneqq\sum_{k=1}^{N^{\prime}}\sum_{x\in\{0,1\}^{n}}\sqrt{\mu_{k}/2^{n}}\ket{x}\ket{u_{k}}\otimes\ket{x}\overline{\ket{u_{k}}},

(5.12)

then,

\lvert\langle\alpha|\beta\rangle\rvert\geq\real\,\langle\alpha|\beta\rangle=\frac{1}{2}(\lVert\ket{\alpha}\rVert^{2}+\lVert\ket{\beta}\rVert^{2}-\lVert\ket{\alpha}-\ket{\beta}\rVert^{2})\geq 1-\epsilon/2.

(5.13)

Now,
\ket{\alpha}
is normalized (because
\sum_{i}\lambda_{i}=1
) and has Schmidt rank
N
, and the Schmidt coefficients of
\ket{\beta}
are each at most
\sqrt{\mu_{k}/2^{n}}\leq 2^{-n/2}
. Therefore, by the above observation,

N\geq(1-\epsilon/2)^{2}/(2^{-n/2})^{2}\geq(1-\epsilon)\,2^{n},

(5.14)

which completes the proof.
∎

Remark
.

Some ideas behind our proof of
Proposition

5.6

come from
[
JNV+22
, Proof of Theorem 8.3]
.

We now use
Proposition

5.6
to prove the main theorem of this section. Much of the proof is devoted to bookkeeping to ensure that the (normalized) density operator condition in
Proposition

5.6
is satisfied and that we are bounding the
quantum
dimension.

Theorem 5.7
.

Let
D
be an efficient device with Hilbert space
