\mathcal{H}=\mathcal{H}_{D}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{Y}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{R}
. Let the classical-quantum decomposition of
\mathcal{H}
be
\mathcal{H}_{C}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{Q}
, so that all states and observables of
D
on
\mathcal{H}
are classical on
\mathcal{H}_{C}
, i.e., block-diagonal in a fixed basis
\{\ket{c}\mid c\in[\dim(\mathcal{H}_{C})]\}
of
\mathcal{H}_{C}
. If
D
can pass the dimension test protocol of
Fig.

7
with probability
\geq 1-\epsilon
, then the quantum dimension of
D
,
\dim(H_{Q})
, is at least
(1-O(N^{2}\epsilon^{1/32}))2^{N}
.

Proof.

It suffices to assume
\epsilon=O(1/N^{64})
, else the bound on
\dim(H_{Q})
holds vacuously as
N\rightarrow\infty
. In this proof, we use
\mathrm{poly}(N,\epsilon)
to mean a polynomial of order
O(N^{2}\epsilon^{1/32})
for convenience.

We model
D
in a way that is analogous to
Section

4.1
, which defines states
\sigma^{\theta}\in\mathcal{H}_{D}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{Y}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{R}
and projective measurements
P_{0^{N}}
and
P_{1^{N}}
corresponding to the device’s second answer when asked questions
q=0^{N},1^{N}
respectively. By following the arguments in
Section

4
, we can prove the following analogue of
Eq.

4.191
in
4.40
:

\sum_{v\in\{0,1\}^{N}}\sum_{u\in\{0,1\}^{N}}\|\mathcal{V}P_{1^{N}}^{u}\sigma^{0,v}P_{1^{N}}^{u}\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}-2^{-N}|-^{u}\rangle\langle-^{u}|\otimes\alpha^{0,v}\|_{1}\leq\mathrm{poly}(N,\epsilon),

(5.15)

where
\ket{-^{u}}\coloneqq H^{\otimes N}|u\rangle\langle u|H^{\otimes N}
.

In the following, for
v\in\{0,1\}^{N}
, we write
\sigma^{v}\coloneqq\sigma^{0,v}
and
\alpha^{v}\coloneqq\alpha^{0,v}
for convenience.
Let
S\coloneqq\{v\in\{0,1\}^{N}\mid\mathrm{Tr}[\sigma^{v}]>0\}
. For
v\in S
, we write
\hat{\sigma}^{v}\coloneqq\sigma^{v}/\mathrm{Tr}[\sigma^{v}]
and
\tilde{\alpha}^{v}\coloneqq\alpha^{v}/\mathrm{Tr}[\sigma^{v}]
. Then,

\sum_{v\in S}\mathrm{Tr}[\sigma^{v}]\Bigl\lVert\mathcal{V}\Bigl(\sum_{u\in\{0,1\}^{N}}P_{1^{N}}^{u}\hat{\sigma}^{v}P_{1^{N}}^{u}\Bigr)\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}-2^{-N}\sum_{u\in\{0,1\}^{N}}|-^{u}\rangle\langle-^{u}|\otimes\tilde{\alpha}^{v}\Bigr\rVert_{1}\leq\mathrm{poly}(N,\epsilon).

(5.16)

Note that
\sum_{u\in\{0,1\}^{N}}|-^{u}\rangle\langle-^{u}|=\mathbbm{1}
.

Let
v_{\min}
be the
v\in S
that minimizes
\|\mathcal{V}(\sum_{u\in\{0,1\}^{N}}P_{1^{N}}^{u}\hat{\sigma}^{v}P_{1^{N}}^{u})\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}-2^{-N}\mathbbm{1}\otimes\tilde{\alpha}^{v}\|_{1}
. Then,
Lemma

4.13
gives

\Bigl\lVert\mathcal{V}\Bigl(\sum_{u\in\{0,1\}^{N}}P_{1^{N}}^{u}\hat{\sigma}^{v_{\min}}P_{1^{N}}^{u}\Bigr)\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}-2^{-N}\mathbbm{1}\otimes\tilde{\alpha}^{v_{\min}}\Bigr\rVert_{1}\leq\mathrm{poly}(N,\epsilon)/(1-\gamma_{P})\leq\mathrm{poly}(N,\epsilon),

(5.17)

where the last inequality holds by
Proposition

4.7
and
\epsilon=O(1/N)
.

Note that
\tilde{\alpha}^{v_{\min}}
is not necessarily normalized, but we can show
\mathrm{Tr}[\tilde{\alpha}^{v_{\min}}]
is close to
1

as follows.
To simplify notation, let
\sigma\coloneqq\hat{\sigma}^{v_{\min}}
and
\tilde{\alpha}\coloneqq\tilde{\alpha}^{v_{\min}}
. Then,

\displaystyle\lvert 1-\mathrm{Tr}[\tilde{\alpha}]\rvert=

\displaystyle\Bigl|\Bigl\lVert\mathcal{V}\Bigl(\sum_{u\in\{0,1\}^{N}}P_{1^{N}}^{u}\sigma P_{1^{N}}^{u}\Bigr)\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}\Bigr\rVert_{1}-\|2^{-N}\mathbbm{1}\otimes\tilde{\alpha}\|_{1}\Bigr|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\Bigl\lVert\mathcal{V}\Bigl(\sum_{u\in\{0,1\}^{N}}P_{1^{N}}^{u}\sigma P_{1^{N}}^{u}\Bigr)\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}-2^{-N}\mathbbm{1}\otimes\tilde{\alpha}\Bigr\rVert_{1}\leq\mathrm{poly}(N,\epsilon).

Now, let
\alpha\coloneqq\tilde{\alpha}/\mathrm{Tr}[\tilde{\alpha}]
. Note that
\mathrm{Tr}[\alpha]=\mathrm{Tr}[\sigma]=1
.
Then,

\displaystyle\Bigl\lVert\mathcal{V}\Bigl(\sum_{u\in\{0,1\}^{N}}P_{1^{N}}^{u}\sigma P_{1^{N}}^{u}\Bigr)\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}-2^{-N}\mathbbm{1}\otimes\alpha\Bigr\rVert_{1}

(5.18)

\displaystyle\leq\Bigl\lVert\mathcal{V}\Bigl(\sum_{u\in\{0,1\}^{N}}P_{1^{N}}^{u}\sigma P_{1^{N}}^{u}\Bigr)\mathcal{V}^{\dagger}-2^{-N}\mathbbm{1}\otimes\mathrm{Tr}[\tilde{\alpha}]\alpha\Bigr\rVert_{1}+\|2^{-N}\mathbbm{1}\otimes\mathrm{Tr}[\tilde{\alpha}]\alpha-2^{-N}\mathbbm{1}\otimes\alpha\|_{1}\leq\mathrm{poly}(N,\epsilon).

Let
\rho\coloneqq\sum_{u\in\{0,1\}^{N}}P_{1^{N}}^{u}\sigma P_{1^{N}}^{u}
. From the definition of
\mathcal{V}
in
Fig.

3
, we see
that there exists a unitary
U\in\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{C}^{2^{n}}\otimes\mathcal{H})
of the form

U=\sum_{c=1}^{\dim\mathcal{H}_{C}}|c\rangle\langle c|\otimes U_{c},

(5.19)

where each
U_{c}\in\mathcal{L}(\mathbb{C}^{2^{n}}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{Q})
is unitary, such that

\displaystyle\|U(|0\rangle\langle 0|^{\otimes N}\otimes\rho)U^{\dagger}-2^{-N}\mathbbm{1}\otimes\alpha\|_{1}\leq\mathrm{poly}(N,\epsilon).

(5.20)

Here, we used the fact that the controlled-
Z_{i}
and controlled-
X_{i}
gates appearing in
\mathcal{V}
are both block-diagonal in the
\{|c\rangle\langle c|\}_{c}
basis because
Z_{i}
and
X_{i}
are observables of
D
on
\mathcal{H}
.

We can also write
\rho
and
\alpha
as

\rho=\sum_{c=1}^{\dim\mathcal{H}_{C}}|c\rangle\langle c|\otimes\rho_{c}\quad\text{and}\quad\alpha=\sum_{c=1}^{\dim\mathcal{H}_{C}}|c\rangle\langle c|\otimes\alpha_{c},

(5.21)

where
\rho_{c},\alpha_{c}\in\mathrm{Pos}(\mathcal{H}_{Q})
are such that
\sum_{c}\mathrm{Tr}[\rho_{c}]=\sum_{c}\mathrm{Tr}[\alpha_{c}]=1
.

Then,
Eq.

5.20
implies

\sum_{c=1}^{\dim\mathcal{H}_{C}}\|U_{c}(|0\rangle\langle 0|^{\otimes N}\otimes\rho_{c})U_{c}^{\dagger}-2^{-N}\mathbbm{1}\otimes\alpha_{c}\|_{1}\leq\mathrm{poly}(N,\epsilon).

(5.22)

Analogously to how we handled the sum over
v\in\{0,1\}^{N}
in
Eq.

5.15
, we can use
Eq.

5.22
to show that
there exists some
c^{\star}\in[\dim\mathcal{H}_{C}]
and (normalized) density operators
\rho^{\star}
and
\alpha^{\star}
on
\mathcal{H}_{Q}
such that

\|U_{c^{\star}}(|0\rangle\langle 0|^{\otimes N}\otimes\rho^{\star})U_{c^{*}}^{\dagger}-2^{-N}\mathbbm{1}\otimes\alpha^{\star}\|_{1}\leq\mathrm{poly}(N,\epsilon).

(5.23)

Finally, we apply
Proposition

5.6
to obtain

\dim\mathcal{H}_{Q}\geq\Rank(\rho^{\star})\geq(1-\mathrm{poly}(N,\epsilon))2^{N},

(5.24)

which completes the proof.
∎

Remark
.

We allow the prover to prepare
\rho
by a procedure that includes measurements and resetting qubits. These may introduce extra classical dimensions to the system but not extra quantum dimensions. Since in our proof, we factor out all the possible classical dimensions (see
Eq.

5.21
) the prover’s measurements and resetting of qubits do not affect our lower bound on the
quantum
dimension.
